# Wave 2+ subplan scope audit — the plan set after the 2026-08-22 rewrite

- Date: 2026-08-23
- Tree audited: `778d11a` on branch `fix/post-audit-code-round`
- Subject: the 15 wave-2+ plan documents rewritten under merge `c0c5627` ("docs(plans): merge the wave-0/1 plan audit", 2026-08-22) — the eleven wave-2 through wave-5 subplans and their four verification checkpoints
- Compared against: the pre-rewrite text of each file at `d361f06` ("chore: initial commit — design document and build plans"); `Qompack.md` as the design of record; `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` as the normative interface spec; the sibling plan set (`README.md`, `TRACEABILITY.md`, `OWNERS.tsv`, `CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv`); and the shipped wave-0/wave-1 tree under `internal/`, `cmd/`, `test/` and `tools/`
- Also in scope as a moving part: the post-audit code-fix round merged at `33bb206`, which landed *after* the plan rewrites, so plan text citing wave-1 behaviour can be one round stale

## Verdict

Yes — the recent changes to the wave-2+ subplans changed their scope, substantially and across every one of the fifteen files. This was not a formatting pass. Obligations moved between subplans, three frozen DAG edge directions were reversed, one subplan gained a prerequisite architecture branch that edits three other packages before its own branch may be cut, another lost its hand-written key derivation because the shipped one is already frozen by a contract fixture, and two plans two waves apart now specify the same file with contradictory function bodies.

The shape of it: **15 files, 425 verified change records, 0 REFUTED** — 363 CONFIRMED as the extractor stated them and 62 ADJUSTED, where the adversarial verifier corrected the kind or the materiality but not the existence of the change. By materiality the split is **211 scope-changing, 163 contract-only, 51 cosmetic**. Ninety-two of the 425 were changes the per-file extractor missed entirely and a verifier caught. Twenty-six records carry a design conflict the verifier ruled real. Four cross-cutting agents plus a completeness critic returned **64 findings — 5 blocker, 14 high, 24 medium, 16 low, 5 informational**; one of the five blockers (`CG-01`) was a coverage complaint against an earlier synthesis pass and does not survive against this evidence set, leaving four live blockers.

The direction of travel is overwhelmingly toward the shipped tree, not away from it. The dominant kind is `contract-changed` (114 records), followed by `obligation-added` (76), `correction-only` (58) and `test-plan-changed` (44); against those, `scope-widened` is 14 and `deferred-to-later` is 3. Most substantive changes are a plan discovering that wave 1 shipped something different from what the plan assumed and re-basing on it: `Options.Bind` instead of `Options.Handle`, `store.ArgsDigest` instead of a local helper, `sketch.ReplaceGenerational` instead of a hand-rolled rename dance, a real frozen `Descriptor.Key` instead of a stub, `internal/observer/observertest` recorded as shipping rather than to be written. Where the rewrite reversed a decision, the old text was usually the one that could not have compiled or would have silently corrupted a frozen fixture — SP-08's `Strip: nil` verbatim-capture request asked `canon.gateSet` for *maximum* stripping while describing it as the minimum, and its Phase-1 exit harness measured a ratio over roughly sixteen bytes per event, which made the gate unpassable and the number meaningless. The cost of that re-basing is a larger obligation surface: pre-step `arch/` branches, new normative sections binding wave-1 carried defects onto wave 2, and exit criteria that are now grep-enforced.

What must be acted on before wave 2 starts is a short list, and it is not the scope changes themselves — those are mostly right. It is four live blockers and the amendments the rewrites skipped. `XS-02`: SP-08 moved the observer onto `Services` seam binding through `WireObserver`, and in the same commit forbade SP-08 from editing `internal/cli/daemon.go:86`, the only non-test `daemon.New` call site in the repo — so nothing calls `WireObserver`, all five seams stay nil at merge, and every hook still exits 0. `CD-1` and `CD-2`: fourteen rows in `plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` are `deferred:V3-VERIFY` while V3-VERIFY names six, and the sign-off guard is keyed on `plans/V3-report.md`, a file V3-VERIFY never tells anyone to write — so the gate is vacuous by construction. `XS-01` is a plan-versus-plan contradiction that detonates in wave 3 and wave 5 rather than wave 2, but the fix belongs in the same pass. Alongside them sit thirteen design conflicts against `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` where a rewritten plan binds itself to a shipped signature the normative spec still spells the old way, with no `arch/<reason>` amendment commit anywhere — precisely the failure §0 calls "the single failure mode that makes parallel waves worthless."

## Method

**What was compared against what.** Each of the fifteen wave-2+ plan documents was diffed from its authoring commit `d361f06` to `HEAD` (`778d11a`), and every hunk was read against four baselines: `Qompack.md`, the read-only design of record, for the obligations the subplan owes; `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` for normative §5 interface signatures, the §3.2 import table, §6.4 coverage floors, §9 branch and merge rules, §10 commit conventions and §13 invariants; the sibling plan documents for cross-subplan contract agreement and for `TRACEABILITY.md` / `OWNERS.tsv` / `README.md` / `CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` consistency; and the shipped Go tree, for whether each claim a plan makes about wave-0 or wave-1 code is actually true of that code.

**A note on the `ARCH-n` labels below.** They are the audit agents' own shorthand and appear in no repository document — `grep -rn 'ARCH-[0-9]' plans/ Qompack.md` matches only this file. Three are used here and each resolves to real normative text: `ARCH-1` is `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` lines 3-5, "Where this document and `Qompack.md` disagree on *what* to build, `Qompack.md` wins"; `ARCH-2` is §0's amendment rule at lines 16-18 and 24-26; `ARCH-4` is the §5 preamble at line 619, "These are the seams. Signatures are normative". Read them as pointers to those lines, not as document-assigned rule ids.

**The commits.** The fifteen files were each rewritten by one commit inside merge `c0c5627`, listed in the table above. `Qompack.md` itself has never been modified: `git log --oneline -- Qompack.md` returns exactly one line, `d361f06`. The post-audit code-fix round merged at `33bb206` landed after the plan rewrites, which is why some plan text describing wave-1 behaviour is one round stale — `CG-11` records that this round touched exactly one line of one wave-2+ plan, so its staleness sweep was not systematic.

**The agent structure.** Thirty-eight agents ran. Per file, one extractor read the current text, `git diff d361f06 HEAD -- <file>`, and the audit rewrite commit, and produced change records. One adversarial verifier then independently re-ran the diffs, checked that every quoted excerpt appears verbatim on the correct side of the diff, re-attributed the design obligation each change touches, corrected materiality where the extractor over- or under-stated it, and searched for changes the extractor had missed. Four cross-cutting agents covered traceability integrity, cross-subplan contract consistency, carried defects and deferred obligations, and wave process obligations and gates for waves 2 through 5; a fifth, a completeness critic, audited what the 15-file-plus-4-area design itself did not cover.

**What 0 REFUTED does and does not prove.** Of 425 extracted changes, none was refuted: no extractor invented a change the diff does not contain, and no quoted excerpt failed verbatim checking. Sixty-two were ADJUSTED — the change is real, the extractor's reading of its kind or its materiality was not — and verifiers caught 92 changes the extractors had missed. So the change *inventory* is trustworthy, and its *classification* was corrected in roughly one case in seven. It does not prove the classifications that survived are right, it does not prove the inventory is complete beyond the 92 recovered misses, and `CG-09` records eight SP-08 verifier-only items confirmed as text changes and never assessed against the design — their `designObligation` and `risk` fields are literally `?` and their `designConflict` is `unchecked`. Those must not be read as adjudicated.

**This is a documentation audit.** It verifies plans against the design of record, the architecture spec, the sibling plan set and the shipped wave-1 tree. It does not execute wave-2+ code, because none exists. Where a finding names a runnable command — a `-run` pattern that matches nothing, a `--json` flag the replay driver rejects, a `--hook checkpoint` argument that exits 2 — that command was run against the shipped tools and the result recorded. Waves 2 through 5 are not implemented, and "the code does not exist yet" is never a finding here.

**Supersession.** A first synthesis pass over this evidence truncated its input: only 1 of the 15 per-file records reached it, every per-file object carrying `"file":"V3-SP-08-observer-l0.md"`, with 14 of the 15 files unrepresented. (`CG-01` sizes that gap as "3,454 of the 4,414 inserted lines". Neither number reproduces: `git diff --shortstat c0c5627^1 c0c5627` over the fifteen files gives 3,776 insertions and 5,342 changed lines, and the SP-08 rewrite alone is 732 insertions / 960 changed lines. The gap's *shape* — one file of fifteen — is what the finding rests on, and that is confirmed.) The document previously at this path was written from that pass and reported 59 classified changes, 4 design conflicts and 39 cross-cutting findings. This document is the re-run on the complete evidence set — `merged.json` carries all 15 per-file keys and `counts.json` records `filesAudited: 15` — and its counts supersede that pass entirely. The earlier pass's coverage complaint against itself is retained below as `CG-01`, downgraded to informational.

## Scope changes by subplan

Fifteen sections, one per audited file, grouped by wave. Each opens with the rewrite commit and its diffstat, then a prose account of what the rewrite did and why, then a table of every scope-changing and contract-only record: its id, its kind, what changed, the design obligation it touches, and the risk if it goes unnoticed. Cosmetic and correction-only records are counted at the foot of each section rather than tabled. The record ids in these tables are the citation keys used by the design-conflict and cross-cutting sections below.

## Wave 2 (group V3) — SP-08, SP-09, V3-VERIFY

Three plan files, 93 verified change records, 0 REFUTED — 43 scope-changing, 42 contract-only, 8 cosmetic. Seven records carry a design conflict the verifier ruled real. This is the wave that starts next, so it is also the wave whose defects are due now rather than later: three of the four live blockers land here (`XS-02` in SP-08, `CD-1` and `CD-2` in V3-VERIFY; the fourth, `XS-01`, is a wave-3/wave-5 collision), and the largest single rewrite in the set — `0380e66`, +732/−228 — is SP-08's.

### V3-SP-08-observer-l0.md

Rewrite commit `0380e66` — "docs(v3-sp-08): rewrite the observer plan against the shipped seams" (732 insertions / 228 deletions). Whole range `d361f06..HEAD`: 791 insertions / 228 deletions across four commits (`0380e66`, `b7d9654` +55, `fe68485` +4, `9d0b078` 1/1).

The rewrite re-based the plan on what SP-01 and SP-05–SP-07 actually shipped, and the re-basing cut both ways. Work left SP-08: the seven NodeID constructors and the hand-rolled §8.1 item-4 edge emission go to `dag.Build*`/`dag.*Node`, `argsDigestAndPreview`/`argsPreviewMax` go to `store.ArgsDigest`, `internal/observer/observertest` is recorded as shipping rather than to be created, and the five `Options.Handle` op registrations become five `Options.Bind` seams on `Services`. Work arrived: a pre-step `arch/sp08-observer-seams` branch whose Commit 0 edits `internal/store/put.go`, `internal/cli/hookclient.go`, `internal/daemon/handlers.go` and 00-ARCHITECTURE §5.8 before `feat/sp08-observer-l0` may be cut, and a new normative section binding three wave-1 carried defects. Three frozen edge directions were reversed to match SP-07 D-1 and the frozen `graph-basic.jsonl` — `EdgeSupersedes` newer→older becomes older→newer, segment membership `segment→member` becomes `member→segment`, subagent consume `capture→tooluse` becomes `toolresult→capture` — and the persisted `state/observer.json` schema changed key-for-key. The Phase-1 exit harness was rebuilt to drive real bytes from `testdata/corpora/toolout/` because `eval.Synthesize` emits `call.Result = {"tokens":N}` and `args: null`, which makes `ratioOn == ratioOff` and leaves `pathKey` empty on every event.

**Design conflict — SP-08-C2 (`Options.Bind` is not in §5).** `grep -c "Bind" plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns **0**. §5.4's extension-seam note names only three seams — *"Handle registers an Op handler; the op-routing table is data, not a switch. / IdleController.Register adds O3/O5 background work. / Services is the late-bound dependency set"* — and freezes exactly one wiring signature at line 807, `func (*Options) Handle(op ipc.Op, h ipc.Handler)`. The rewritten plan makes an undeclared method and an undeclared field set SP-08's sole wiring mechanism and calls it *"the sanctioned seam"*, while Commit 0's checklist amends only *"the §5.8 note in `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md`"*. ARCH-4: "Signatures are normative"; §0: "Silent divergence from §5 is the single failure mode that makes parallel waves worthless." The pre-step is one bullet short.

**Design conflict — SP-08-C6 (`ArgsDigest` is bound to a §5.8 that does not carry it).** The plan states *"§5.8 ships `ArgsDigest(raw json.RawMessage) (core.Hash, string)`"* and lists it inside its block of `store.Store` interface methods. §5.8's `Store` interface declares no such method: the only occurrence of the name in the whole file is the struct field `ArgsDigest   core.Hash` at line 988, and the shipped symbol is a package-level function (`internal/store/tooluseindex.go:348`). §5.8 also spells the cap in characters — `ArgsPreview  string          // ≤120 chars, for tombstones and \`timeline\`` (line 989) — while the plan and the shipped code both cap at 120 **bytes**. The pre-step amends §5.8's `PutOptions` note and touches neither divergence.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 2 changes (SP-08-C26, SP-08-C27) — a commit-sha citation remapped onto the rewritten history (`260dbab` → `666b120`, verified as "test(canon): pin a non-ANSI deletion-mediated limit (SP04-D2)") and the recommended-model header added by `fe68485`.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**
- The `Bind` seam has no §5.4 declaration and Commit 0 schedules no amendment for it — the missing bullet quantified above under SP-08-C2.
- **A resolved decision was rewritten** — a class of edit plans/README.md:60 treats as load-bearing. Resolved decision 3 changes on two axes: "(a) the prompt bytes stored **uncanonicalized** … via `store.PutBytes`" → "(a) … with **no optional canonicalization class and no MinHash**", and "(c) a `KindUserPrompt` DAG node anchored … by an `EdgeSequence` from `segment:<id>`" → "(c) … built by `dag.BuildUserPrompt` … running `userprompt:<turn> → segment:<id>` — members point **into** the segment".
- Three of SP-08's named tests are authored in *other subplans' packages*: `TestRawExtras_ResolvesTheSubagentNameClientSide` (`internal/cli`), `TestResolveEvent_RestoresRawExtras` (`internal/daemon`) and the extended `TestDecodeRequestRoundTrip` (`internal/ipc`).
- `graph_test.go` and `supersede_test.go` were rebuilt, not edited: graph_test loses six rows and gains nine, three of which assert properties of SP-07's shipped builder; supersede_test swaps two `PrevOnPath` rows out for `_ToolUsesByPathIsMostRecentFirst` and `_EmitsNoEdgesItself`.
- The TDD procedure itself was qualified for the partly-shipped package: Commit 1 now confirms the **new** rows fail "while `TestHumanBytes_UsesTheBinaryDivisor` and `TestTombstone_IsAddressable` still pass."
- Four file-section headings were re-labelled "(new)" → "(exists …)" — `doc.go`, `observer.go`, `signals.go` — with the new constraint that `Options` is *widened*, not renamed (Rule W-3).
- `ensureSegment` gained a new ordering dependency: `loadState` must now run before it, or every resumed segment records `StartPos` 0.
- The Phase-1 gate is now pinned to `test/dedup`'s `testrunnerGainFloor` (also 1.25), a constant in SP-04's test tree. Related: the criterion's "off" arm does not actually disable stripping in shipped code — `canonOptions` leaves `opts.Strip` nil when canonicalization is off (`internal/store/put.go:242-248`) and `canon.gateSet` reads nil as every class, so both arms may strip identically. Pre-step (a) does not fix it.

### V3-SP-09-negative-knowledge.md

Rewrite commit `de7f7b1` — "docs(v3-sp-09): bind the plan to the frozen fixtures and the wave-1 carries" (279 insertions / 113 deletions). Whole range `d361f06..HEAD`: 335 insertions / 109 deletions across four commits (`de7f7b1`, `b7d9654` +56, `fe68485` +4, `9d0b078` 1/1).

`b7d9654` and `de7f7b1` added a normative "Inherited constraints from wave 1" section carrying three binding obligations that did not exist before: SP04-D2/D3 (nothing persisted may bake in a canonical-form hash it cannot recompute), SP05-D1 (the drain is not lossless, so `RefreshStaleness` must stay re-runnable and idempotent), and SP-07's INHERIT — the dependence graph is legitimately cyclic, so the detector needs a written termination argument and a cyclic-fixture test. `de7f7b1` then re-based the implementation surface on what wave 1 shipped: `Descriptor.Key`, the `Record` struct and the Scope/Status/SourceKind constants are already on develop and frozen by contract fixtures, so the plan deletes its own — materially different — `Key()` implementation and its `record.golden.json`, handing byte authority to `testdata/golden/contracts/negknow/want/elimination_record.jsonl`. That fixture forced three wire-format reversals: `source` is an integer not a string, a log line is a bare `Record` not a `{"op":"add","rec":…}` envelope, and the id prefix is `elim_` not `elm_`. Persistence moved wholesale to SP-03 — the hand-rolled §3.3 tmp-stage/rename/`.bak`/state-file procedure is replaced by one `sketch.ReplaceGenerational` call with the counter seeded from `paths.HighestBloomBackupSeq`, and `.qompack/state/` is struck from the allowed write set. Net: smaller implementation surface, larger obligation surface.

Evidence note: SP-09-C02 and SP-09-C07 both cite `plans/V3-VERIFY-observer-negative-knowledge-and-l0.md`, which does not exist; the intended file is `plans/V3-VERIFY-observer-and-negative-knowledge.md`. The substance of both records was verified against the real file.

**Design conflict — SP-09-C09 (`paths` signatures corrected in the plan, never in §3.3).** 00-ARCHITECTURE §3.3 line 492 still reads *"`paths.CreateNew(p)` uses `O_EXCL`. Checkpoint files use it"* (one argument) and line 508 *"**Atomic writes.** `paths.WriteAtomic(p, b)` writes to `.qompack/tmp/<rand>`, `Sync()`, then `os.Rename` onto `p`"* (two arguments). The plan now specifies `func CreateNew(p string, b []byte) error` and `func WriteAtomic(p string, b []byte, perm fs.FileMode) error`, matching the shipped `internal/paths/appendonly.go:114` and `internal/paths/atomic.go:105`. The plan matches the code and 00-ARCHITECTURE does not; ARCH-2 requires an `arch/<reason>` amendment for that, and none exists.

**Design conflict — SP-09-C10 and SP-09-C21 (§5.7 says `Load` logs; the shipped `Load` does not).** 00-ARCHITECTURE §5.7 line 905 declares `func Load(p string, s Sketch) error   // CRC + version checked; corrupt → ErrNotFound + Loud log`. The rewritten plan says the opposite — *"`func Load(p string, s Sketch) error   // logs nothing — NOT for this call site`"* and *"`Load` runs on a `logging.Nop()` and writes no line anywhere"* — which is what `internal/sketch/io.go:95` does. Further, `grep -n 'ReplaceGenerational\|LoadWithLog\|HighestBloomBackupSeq\|ReplaceBloom' plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns nothing: §5.7 lists neither `LoadWithLog` nor `ReplaceGenerational`, yet the plan now makes both mandatory. Under ARCH-4 ("Signatures are normative … may not change or remove anything below without an amendment") this seam moved without an amendment landing on develop.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 2 changes (SP-09-C01, SP-09-C05) — the recommended-model header from `fe68485` and the same `260dbab` → `666b120` citation remap from `9d0b078`.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**
- The `Descriptor` JSON codec gained two new binding requirements in the Interface contract → Produces block: the hand-written codec must emit bytes *identical to what SP-01's shipped struct tags produce*, and a missing or null `symbol` must decode to `""`.
- The same hunk re-labelled the two function comments at the normative interface surface — `Canonicalize` gains "// SP-01 stub body; SP-09 fills it in place" and `Key()` gains "// ALREADY SHIPPED AND FROZEN by SP-01 — do not re-derive" — a location neither C13 nor C26 cites.
- The `ledger` struct gained `lay paths.Layout` (plan line 949), the state-carrying half of C20/C21/C22, reported nowhere.
- A second test row was tightened besides the one C28 names: `TestSourceKind_RoundTrip` gains "`SourceSlashCommand == 1`, matching the two frozen fixtures' `"source":1`".
- Two bloom test rows encode the new persistence contract and are omitted from C28: `TestRebuildBloom_Persistence` drops the `state/negknow.json` assertion and gains the **incoming**-seq backup naming (`tried.bloom.1.bak`) plus "**no `state/` file is created**"; `TestRebuildBloom_OneBakGeneration` gains "its seq strictly increasing". These are the only assertions pinning the naming C22's risk analysis calls load-bearing.
- A package-wide grep obligation was added in `record.go`: "no golden byte anywhere contains `"source":"mcp"`".
- The `eliminations.golden.jsonl` fixture row acquired a *positional* constraint stronger than C18's quoted text: "The **first** record line is `want/elimination_record.jsonl`'s single line copied verbatim".
- A worked-example literal in `RefreshStaleness` was re-based on the new fixture (`sha256:11aa22bb33cc` → `sha256:1a4d7c0f3b6e`).

### V3-VERIFY-observer-and-negative-knowledge.md

Rewrite commit `b25d8d8` — "docs(v3-verify): resolve the merge order and carry B-G into wave 2" (243 insertions / 82 deletions). Whole range `d361f06..HEAD`: 299 insertions / 74 deletions across five commits (`b25d8d8`, `7362942`, `6bc7aae`, `c3cb115`, `fe68485`).

Three earlier commits built §0a from nothing — `7362942` its first four inbound obligations, `6bc7aae` item 5 (the deferred carried-defect rows), `c3cb115` the sixth row. `b25d8d8` then made four scope-changing moves: it flipped the wave-2 merge order in §0 from SP-08→SP-09 to SP-09→SP-08 and closed §0a item 4 RESOLVED, deleting both the "fix the losing document BEFORE the first wave-2 merge" obligation and its false premise that the observer imports negknow; it added §0a item 6, a new obligation to wire B-G/B-D into a production judge or re-carry them by name; it re-scoped §7.1 and §7.2 from "group A *is* the V1 inventory" to "run V1-VERIFY §1's ~165 rows and V2-VERIFY §2 in full", an order-of-magnitude widening; and it rewrote the Phase-1 exit-criterion procedure across §3.8/H15/X6/X12 so the dedup ratio is measured over real corpus bytes, adding two harness-honesty guards. The rest is correction of drifted test names, moved fixture paths, and two wrong driver flags. Two obligations the rewrite did not pick up remain open (V3-C32, V3-C33).

**Design conflict — V3-C4 (README and V3-VERIFY now agree on the merge order and disagree on why).** plans/README.md line 51, rewritten three minutes earlier at `1af5141`, justifies the wave-2 order with a premise this rewrite refutes: *"They follow from the consuming relationships each subplan's own prerequisites section states — SP-09's elimination ledger lands before SP-08's observer, **which imports it** (§3.2)"*. The new §0a item 4 says the opposite: *"`internal/observer` does **not** import `internal/negknow` (SP-08's own exit criteria forbid the import, and `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.2's allow-table … merely *permits* the edge; a permission is not a dependency)"*. The tree agrees with the V3 file — `grep -rn negknow internal/observer/` returns only the `doc.go:8` comment — so README:51's stated reason for its own merge order is known-false and was left standing.

**Design conflict — V3-C5b (README mandates a wave-2 build-order guard; this checkpoint names none).** The same README line 51 states: *"**Every wave from 2 onward adds its own build-order guard to `test/guards/buildorder_test.go` before its branches merge**, written and observed failing against the wrong order first, exactly as `TestGuard_Phase0BeforeStore` was; the wave's verification checkpoint names that guard. A wave that merges without one has an unverified order and must say so in its checkpoint report."* V3-VERIFY names no wave-2 guard, requires none to be authored, and its §10 completion report has no cell for the declaration. Instead §0a item 4 cites the guard file's contents as evidence that nothing constrains the order: *"and `test/guards/buildorder_test.go` holds no wave-2 guard at all; its six guards are `TestGuard_Phase0BeforeStore`, `TestGuard_StoreAndNegknowBeforeCheckpoint`, `TestGuard_SubmodularInertWithoutPSelection`, `TestGuard_SelectorRefusesWithoutPSelection`, `TestGuard_O1FlagDefaults` and `TestGuard_SubmodularDefaultsOff`."* Those six names match the shipped file exactly (lines 22, 35, 47, 59, 95, 112). Both documents were rewritten inside the same merge, `c0c5627`.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 4 changes (V3-C12, V3-C13, V3-C29, V3-C31) — drifted-name and unsatisfiable-assertion repairs plus the model header. Individually cosmetic, V3-C12 alone corrects the `-run` patterns and asserted names of B3, B4, B5, B10, D1, D3 and D4; every pre-rewrite name it replaced is dead in the tree, so those rows were vacuous ok-prints, and D4's own new text says the row "passed vacuously before".

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**
- §3.1's Phase-0 procedure is a **fourth** site where the six-budget contract became seven ("confirm `obs.Budgets()` returns **seven** entries, B-A…B-G"), covered by no claim — and it is the site inside the section that actually grades the §11.3 guardrails, so it is the one a session executes.
- The seventh budget is institutionalized across four sites with no 00-ARCHITECTURE amendment required anywhere. §2.4 (lines 182-195) is headed *"**Latency budgets (normative — these are what CI gates on).**"* and lists exactly six rows, B-A…B-F. The plan now requires `len(obs.Budgets()) == 7` and adds a B-G row to §6.1 and to the completion report. ARCH-2 requires an `arch/<short-reason>` amendment for a change to the normative document; a checkpoint that greens this leaves the table and the shipped `obs.Budgets()` permanently out of step.
- §3.6's Belady procedure command was corrected in the same commit, outside V3-C12's stated scope: `TestOraclePolicy_ScoresExactlyOne` → `TestOraclePolicy_DelegatesToBelady`. The old name resolves nowhere, so §3.6 — an exit-criterion procedure — silently dropped one of three alternates and the "oracle must score exactly 1.0" assertion had no test behind it.
- B4 gained a net-new named assertion, not only renames: `TestStockPolicy_BudgetSmallerThanOneBlockTerminates` appears nowhere on the removed line, and the dropped `TestStockPolicy_PIsZero` is folded into a parenthetical on `_TopFiveFilesFiveKEach`, so a previously independent assertion now holds only as a side effect of another test.

## Wave 3 (group V4) — SP-10, SP-11, SP-12, SP-13, V4-VERIFY

Five plan files, 146 verified change records, 0 REFUTED. 129 of them are scope-changing or contract-only and are tabled below; 15 are cosmetic. Seven records carry a design conflict the verifier ruled real — six of them in SP-11.

### V4-SP-10-checkpointer-l4.md

Rewrite commit `74c2c58` — *docs(v4-sp-10): stop the plan re-declaring frozen types and stealing a route* — 431 insertions, 188 deletions. Total drift `d361f06..HEAD` is 435/188 across one file; the extra four lines are `fe68485`'s recommended-model header.

The rewrite's dominant move is ownership. The §5.14/§8.5 checkpoint and pins type set, the `checkpoints/MANIFEST.jsonl` layer, `core.Hash`'s JSON methods, the `qompack checkpoint` thin client and the `ipc.OpCheckpoint` route are all reassigned to SP-01, SP-05 and `internal/paths` as already shipped, so the file map drops from "nineteen new files" to fourteen new plus seven modified and §17 changes from MODIFIED to NO CHANGE REQUIRED. On top of that it changes contracts SP-11, SP-12 and SP-13 will build against: `checkpoint.Filename` is deleted, `pins.Open` keeps its shipped one-argument arity beside a new `OpenWith`, the pins log verb changes `del`→`remove` with the invariant nested and a record-level `ts` to match the frozen Rule W-2 fixtures, the manifest `created` field flips from an RFC 3339 string to a millisecond number, `WireCheckpoint` splits into `BindCheckpoint` + `WireCheckpoint`, and the daemon-internal PreCompact deadline moves from 16 s to 14 s so it nests inside the shipped 15 s `checkpointReplyDeadline`. Two behavioural contracts flip outright — `.qompack/state/precompact.json` is demoted from "the observable for both PreCompact contract assertions" to a debug artifact nothing in `internal/contract` reads, and degraded-passive goes from "still writes the checkpoint" to "the seam is not called at all". Finally the §10 Phase 3 exit criterion is replaced wholesale: the two-run replay comparison at a 30% ResidualSpan threshold is retired as unrunnable (`test/replay/main.go` registers no `--filter`, `--set` or `--json`, and no registered policy reads `config.Checkpoint`) and a new in-suite `TestFrontierAdvanceCutsResidualSpan` gate at 70% becomes the pass/fail condition.

All 32 SP-10 records were ruled `designConflict: none`. Two of those rulings carry information worth keeping: SP-10-C29 **resolves** a conflict — the pre-rewrite text asserted "§5.22 lists `checkpointtest` and no `pinstest` … `internal/pins` has no conformance subpackage of its own", which contradicts both the shipped tree and SP-01's own plan (`V1-SP-01…:419`, `:2207` name `pinstest`) — and SP-10-C4 notes a pre-existing citation defect the rewrite did not introduce: Qompack.md has no §12.1 (its §12 at line 1172 is the risk register), so every "§12.1" in this plan resolves to `00-ARCHITECTURE.md`.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 1 record (SP-10-C32) — `Reader.Get`'s mismatch logging corrected from a package-level call to a method on the logger, the obligation byte-identical on both sides.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- `internal/paths` signature corrections — the largest unfiled scope change. `WriteAtomic` now takes a required `perm fs.FileMode`, `AppendOnly` returns `io.WriteCloser` not `*os.File`, `CreateNew` takes the bytes and does Write+Sync+Close+Chmod itself; plus new `AppendJSONL`, `Long`, `Layout`/`Of`. It propagates into four unfiled section rewrites (§2 Materialize, §7 draft persistence, §13 Finalize step 5 collapsing to one `paths.CreateNew` call, §15 step 6). Code written to the old three signatures does not compile.
- `Ref.Frontier` consumer rule inverted, aimed squarely at SP-11: the recommended source moves from `state/precompact.json` to `store.SegmentLog.Frontier`, with a new prohibition — "it may not read a `Reader`-returned `Ref.Frontier`". `Ref.Path` also moves to `paths.CheckpointPath(l, seq)`.
- Panic-isolation obligation reassigned to shipped code: the recover-and-log duty is deleted from the op handler and all three idle functions and delegated to SP-05's `idleController.runTask`, which already recovers per task.
- A new fixture SP-10 must author: `testdata/golden/contracts/checkpoint/input/oversized_checkpoint.json`, declared `record-by-owner` in the MANIFEST with SP-10 as owner and not present on disk. An added deliverable, not a narrowing.
- New normative rule on how Rule W-1 skips are flipped: each suite guards on a `skipIfStub*` probe that flips itself off; the exact message `"behaviour: implementation is a stub (Rule W-1)"` is grepped by `devtool lint`'s `stubskips` and must never be paraphrased. This inverts the old commit-1 instruction to "flip the `t.Skip`s".
- File-pointer tool resolution given a mechanism: `src.Graph.In(dag.FileNode(pathKey))` resolving the edge's `From` through `src.Graph.Node`, where the old text named no API.
- `nomagic` scope silently retargeted in the exit criteria — the §11.6 forbidden-literal check narrows from "the two files elsewhere" to `internal/daemon/wire_checkpoint.go` alone, with a parallel unfiled edit to the Done checklist's placeholder scan.

### V4-SP-11-rehydrator-l5.md

Rewrite commit `e18aaac` — *docs(v4-sp-11): drop Store.Search from the intent path and bind, not handle* — 400 insertions, 235 deletions. Total drift `d361f06..HEAD` is 404/235; `fe68485` contributes the four-line header.

The rewrite moves scope in both directions. It removes two whole obligations — minting and publishing a §12.1 `additionalContext` sentinel, and adding a `Rehydrator` member to `daemon.Services` — and reassigns them to already-shipped SP-05 code, replacing them with one new obligation, `BindRehydrate` on the existing `Services.Rehydrate` seam. It replaces the mechanism behind §8.6 item 2: a `Store.Search`-plus-session-filter algorithm becomes a single `Store.ToolUse` lookup on a derived `prompt_<session>_0` id, which creates a hard dependency on an SP-08 naming convention that `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` does not record. It hardens the budget contract in three places at once — `Request.Budget` becomes a cap that may be lowered but never raised, min-fill runs only for an unset request, tier-1 material may now be dropped rather than emitted over the cap — and deletes the synthetic `ItemStat{Kind:"overhead"}` row in favour of Σ`Items` == `Result.Tokens`. It adds an entirely new prerequisite step 0: a §0 amendment on `arch/rehydrate-item-kinds` that edits `00-ARCHITECTURE` §5.15, `internal/rehydrate/types.go` and SP-01's `rehydratetest/behaviour.go` before the feature branch is cut. And it rewrites the Phase 3 exit criterion: one policy instead of two, A2 compared against the in-run `stock` arm rather than SP-02's committed Phase 0 baseline artifact, the check relocated from a `_test.go` into the driver binary's `phaseChecks` map, and `KeepSet.IDs` re-specified as `eval.Blocks` block ids.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C12 (`NestedClaudeMD` ancestor walk).** Qompack.md §8.6:971 reads verbatim: "- Every nested `CLAUDE.md` in a directory containing a pointer-set file". `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.15:1603 — normative per ARCH-1, and untouched by this rewrite — reads verbatim: "// NestedClaudeMD returns CLAUDE.md files in directories containing a pointer-set file." Both state the narrow reading. The plan now says at `V4-SP-11-rehydrator-l5.md:922`: "§8.6 says: *"Every nested `CLAUDE.md` in a directory containing a pointer-set file."* The **shipped** contract is broader, and it is the one that binds … **Walk ancestors.**" Its only authority is SP-01's implementation doc comment (`internal/rules/rules.go:32-33`, "containing (**or ancestor to**)") and the inherited `rulestest` fixture. ARCH-1 states "Where this document and `Qompack.md` disagree on *what* to build, `Qompack.md` wins", and no §0 amendment to §5.15 and no PR against Qompack.md is recorded anywhere in the diff. The asymmetry is the defect: the same rewrite opens `arch/rehydrate-item-kinds` to amend §5.15 for a *constant ordering* (SP-11-C10) but silently widens a *behavioural* contract that the same §5.15 block states. The verifier records that the extractor cited only Qompack.md and missed the architecture half.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C24 (Phase 3 A2 comparison basis).** Qompack.md §10 Phase 3:1097 reads verbatim: "**Exit criterion:** post-compaction first-divergence turn index improves against Phase 0 baseline at a *smaller* rehydration budget than stock; measured reduction in summarization-call input tokens when the span instruction is honoured." "Against Phase 0 baseline" is the committed artifact, which `test/replay/phases.go:60` names via `baselinePolicyName = "stock"` and `phase0` computes. The plan's rewritten A2 row (`V4-SP-11-rehydrator-l5.md:1839`) reads: "| **A2 — first-divergence improves** | `c.Driver.Policies[…]["first_divergence_turn"]` for `qompack-rehydrate` vs `stock` — the registered baseline policy, scored in the same run over the same ≥ 20 sessions carrying a `CompactionAt` point | `qompack > stock` …". That is a different quantity: both arms can regress against the recorded Phase 0 number and A2 still passes. The plan argues in-run comparison is "stronger than reading a file" — a defensible engineering position, but not what §10 says, and no amendment or sign-off is recorded.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C17 (tier-1 material becomes droppable).** Qompack.md §8.6:959 reads verbatim "1. **Invariants and pins** — verbatim, always" and :960 "2. **Verbatim original user intent** — from L0, not from any summary (closes G2.3 permanently)". The plan's new step 3 (`V4-SP-11-rehydrator-l5.md:1304`) reads: ""Never truncated" means **never partially emitted** — it does not mean "emitted over the cap". If `overhead + fixed > B`, admit them in `renderOrder` while they fit whole, convert each one that does not fit into a drop entry …". The two documents genuinely disagree, and the shipped wave-0 conformance suite makes the old behaviour impossible to implement — `runBudgetCase` (`internal/rehydrate/rehydratetest/behaviour.go:163-168`) asserts `Tokens <= budget` at budget 1, and `runDegradeCase` (:240-247) repeats it. The rewrite resolves toward the suite; per ARCH-1 the correct resolution was an amendment or a change to the wave-0 case, and neither is recorded. The verifier bounds the tension: reading "never truncated" as "never *partially* emitted" is a defensible construction of §8.5's tier language, and G7.3's residual in Qompack.md §9 already concedes partial closure.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C8 (standing instruction suppressed with item 3).** Qompack.md §8.7:991 reads verbatim: "**Design note:** `already_tried` should be surfaced in the rehydrated context as a *standing instruction*, not merely an available tool. "Before committing to an approach, call `already_tried`." Otherwise the affordance exists and goes unused." That sentence states no condition, so a payload omitting it entirely is in tension with it. `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.15:1594 — normative, unchanged since d361f06 — scopes it: "// StandingInstruction is item 3's companion, emitted verbatim (§8.7 design note):". The plan now says "**When `seen == 0` the item is omitted entirely and the standing instruction is not emitted anywhere.**", deferring to the shipped assertion at `rehydratetest/behaviour.go:224-225` ("the standing instruction belongs to item 3; with no item 3 it must not be emitted"). The verifier's ruling: partial, and a documented pre-existing divergence rather than one this rewrite introduced — the unresolved question is whether "surfaced in the rehydrated context" survives an empty ledger, and nobody has amended either document to say.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C25 (A1 loses the `maxTokens` ceiling).** Partial. Qompack.md §10 Phase 3 requires "at a *smaller* rehydration budget than stock", which A1's first clause still tests. What is lost is the local ceiling check: the deleted clause was "**and** `qompack <= cfg.Runtime.Rehydrate.MaxTokens` on every session", replaced by "`"qompack-rehydrate"` does not appear in `c.Driver.BudgetViolations`" — the harness's per-session replay budget, a different quantity. Qompack.md §8.6:974 says of the 8–12K target "Measure this in the harness before relaxing it", and the harness is now the one place that no longer measures it against `maxTokens`. The plan retains `TestE2E_SessionStartCompactUnderBudget` (≤ 12 000) and the property test, so the obligation is relocated out of the gate rather than dropped.

**Design conflict — SP-11-C28 (`frontierOf` defined in SP-11's driver).** Soft. Qompack.md's O5 (§8.5) assigns continuous frontier advancement to the scheduler/checkpointer pair and Appendix C's `checkpoint.frontier` is SP-10/SP-12 territory; SP-11 defining "the index of the last `Role == "user"` turn at or before `at`" is a local modelling choice inside the replay driver, not a redefinition of the shipped `Ref.Frontier`. The plan says the V4 verification run "replaces `frontierOf` with the real `Ref.Frontier` then" — but no handoff to SP-12 and no row in `plans/V4-VERIFY-*` records that obligation.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 3 records (SP-11-C33, SP-11-C34, SP-11-C35) — a file-map path corrected to the stub's real home `build.go`, the `devtool test`/`lint` invocations corrected to what the tasks actually accept, and `fe68485`'s recommended-model header.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- A stale sibling document, and the concrete casualty C4/C5 only guessed at: `plans/V4-VERIFY-checkpoint-rehydrate-schedule-retrieval.md:294` and `:731` still name `rehydrate.Sentinel(sess, seq)` and the `state/rehydrate-<sess>.json` sentinel key — a function SP-11 no longer produces and a JSON key its state file no longer has. This is not inherited staleness: V4-VERIFY was itself rewritten *after* the SP-11 rewrite (`git merge-base --is-ancestor e18aaac 2504696` succeeds) and these two rows were not corrected. The extractor guessed at SP-05 and SP-14; neither references it.
- An internal inconsistency the rewrite created: the same commit that added "**Not `StandingInstruction`** — SP-01 already shipped it … redeclaring it is a compile error" to the file map left the Produces list intact — line 629 still lists `func StandingInstruction() string`. Three lines from the still-listed `func Build`, so a reader going top-to-bottom gets the opposite instruction.
- C12's conflict is under-stated: the ancestor walk contradicts `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.15:1603 as well as Qompack.md §8.6, and only SP-01's implementation comment supports it. ARCH-2 is arguably engaged and no amendment is opened.
- A new unclaimed obligation in `KeepSet` step 5: `P = 0` with a design justification, added wholesale, inverting the only prior statement about `P` in this plan (the deleted `stockRestorePolicy`'s "`P` is the compaction point's token position").
- A new unclaimed dependency in A3: the row now also asserts `c.Driver.Policies["qompack-rehydrate"]["residual_span_p95"]`, so A3 depends on `eval.ScoreRun` populating the SP-02-owned `Run.ResidualSpan` — with no guard, unlike the block-id zip.
- Three item-2 test rows were **deleted**, not renamed: `TestUserIntent_EarliestTurnOfThisSessionWins`, `TestUserIntent_IgnoresOtherSessions` and `TestUserIntent_ToolUseLookupErrorSkipsHit`. The first two were the only coverage of "never inject another session's prompt" under a multi-hit result set; the replacement covers only the single-record case.
- The hard-cap assertion was strengthened in a way C15/C17 do not capture: old step 9's "`Result.Tokens <= cfg.MaxTokens` … unless `Degraded` is set" becomes "`Result.Tokens <= B` … **unconditionally, `Degraded` or not**", and the matching error-matrix row moves from re-truncating the last **discretionary** item to the last **emitted** item — which is what permits re-truncating a tier-1 item.

### V4-SP-12-scheduler-l3.md

Rewrite commit `121c8ec` — *docs(v4-sp-12): delete the coupling cache and repoint the file map at SP-01* — 277 insertions, 155 deletions. Total drift `d361f06..HEAD` is 281/155; `fe68485` contributes the four-line header.

The rewrite does three things at once. It deletes an obligation: the `CrossingEdges` coupling cache, its `dag.GraphStats` version guard and both its tests, replaced by a live per-candidate call justified against `internal/dag/index.go:120-134` (two `sort.SearchInts` calls, not a graph walk) and a turn→`Pos` cache rekeyed on `maxTurn`, which changes `Assemble`'s signature. It adds a large block of new obligations: SP-12 must delete `internal/scheduler/formulas.go`/`formulas_test.go` and absorb their four shipped symbols with three declared behaviour changes to SP-01-owned code, modify `detector.go`, `types.go` and `schedulertest/behaviour.go`, maintain a new `openSegTokens` accumulator feeding a mandatory `"tokens"` pseudo-feature into `SegmentLog.Close`, run `refreshDecision` once per idle pass (nothing produced a decision on the idle path before), and emit a `sched.frontier.skipped` starvation counter; the idle priority band moves from 10–60 to 110–160 to clear SP-05's three shipped tasks. And it moves the Phase 4 exit criterion: `l3policy`'s pause constants are deleted (0.12 → the shipped 0.15 ms/token from `eval.DefaultLatencyModel()`), a negative-control row proves the three residual rows are one measurement, and the §10 amortization clause is deferred to V4-VERIFY §4.4's live test. Ownership of the daemon store/graph/ledger bootstrap is newly assigned to SP-13, and every reference to `cmd/qompack/main.go` is repointed at `internal/cli/daemon.go`.

**Design conflict — SP-12-C6 (a fabricated architecture obligation).** Not a contradiction with the design, but an invented rule: the composition-root repoint bound `internal/cli/daemon.go` to a 150-line limit sourced to the architecture spec in two places. `plans/V4-SP-12-scheduler-l3.md:2406` now reads "`runDaemon` is ~126 lines today; the three blocks add about 20, which stays inside 00-ARCHITECTURE §3.1's 150-line file bound", and `:2884` requires "`internal/cli/daemon.go` stays under 150 LOC". `grep -n "150" plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns exactly one line — §3.1:306, "│   └── qompack/main.go                   the single binary; dispatch only, <150 LOC" — a bound on the binary's dispatch-only main, not a general file-length rule. The record itself is cosmetic (the repoint from `cmd/qompack/main.go` to `internal/cli/daemon.go` is correct and verified: 30 lines against 126), but the invented obligation is now load-bearing text in a done checklist. The verifier caught it independently; the extractor repeated it rather than catching it.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 4 records (SP-12-C1, C6, C37, C38) — the recommended-model header, the composition-root repoint above, the enumerated test-count bump 173 → 181 (which left a live seven-versus-eight benchmark-baseline inconsistency: `:854` and `:2911` still say "seven" while `:2908` and `:2915` say eight, so one benchmark would ship with no baseline for benchstat to compare), and a Consumes-heading qualifier plus a `DefaultFalse` → `DefaultsFalse` test-name fix that C11's exact-name move requirement depends on.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- A residual contradiction the rewrite left behind, against its own headline change: commit 4's summary paragraph is an unchanged context line and still reads at `:2859` "candidate assembly with a memoized turn→`Pos` map and memoized `CrossingEdges`" — the exact cache `121c8ec` exists to delete. `grep -n "memoized"` returns this one line.
- Cross-plan desync from the state-document rewrite (C26): the golden `p_score` moved −22429.3 → −5379.3, but `V4-VERIFY…:455` row V4-SP12-16 still asserts "`p_score == −22429.3`". The V4 checkpoint now fails by construction against a correct implementation.
- The fabricated 150-LOC obligation above.
- Subagent-strategy work reassignments — an entire changed section never itemised. A2 gains the `formulas.go` deletion and the three godoc corrections and must be told the four symbols already exist ("its job is to move them … not to declare them again"); B1 gains the three `behaviour.go` fixture edits; C1/C2/D1 change target file and evidence obligations. These are subagent fan-out ownership changes, and a stale reading hands A2 a job it will do wrong. (The source record labels them "ARCH-37"; unlike `ARCH-1`/`-2`/`-4` that label resolves to nothing in `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` and is dropped here.)
- A file-map contract change not itemised: `internal/scheduler/evaluate.go` moves from `create` to `modify` — the same class the extractor filed separately for `detector.go` (C12) and `formulas.go` (C11), visible only incidentally inside C33's and C35's quotes.
- Same-wave file collision the repoint creates: SP-12 now adds three blocks to `internal/cli/daemon.go` while SP-13 claims the same file as its exclusive edit (`V4-SP-13…:1373`, "**and nothing else in that file**"). Both insert into the six-line window between `opts := daemon.NewOptions(root, cfg)` (`:81`) and `daemon.New(opts)` (`:86`), both done-checklists assert they are the only outside modification, and order matters — SP-12's Block 1 must run after SP-13's assignments and SP-13's `InstallMCPOp` must be last.

### V4-SP-13-mcp-retrieval-layer.md

Rewrite commit `73dbd4e` — *docs(v4-sp-13): fix the contract observable and the daemon bootstrap* — 73 insertions, 19 deletions. Total drift `d361f06..HEAD` is 77/19; `fe68485` contributes the four-line header. The smallest rewrite in the wave, and the only one whose largest change is a widening.

Two threads dominate. The first is a contract correction: every reference to the `mcp.server_registered` observable is repointed from a struct `contract.History` written to `state/contract.json` onto the shipped `contract.SessionHistory` / `HistoryPath` / pointer-based `LoadHistory`/`SaveHistory` at `state/history.json`, the `Services.MCPInitialized` seam is retyped from `func() bool` to the shipped `func(ctx context.Context) bool`, and the assertion's severity is corrected from `SevWarn` to `SevInfo` with a new instruction not to strengthen it. The second is a genuine scope widening: the daemon bootstrap, previously "gains exactly one line", becomes a full resident-set block that makes SP-13 the owner of opening the store, the DAG, the elimination ledger and the promoter in `internal/cli/daemon.go`, degrading loudly on each failure — an ownership move SP-12's plan was correspondingly corrected to depend on. A third, smaller correction replaces a call to a non-existent `obs.MetricFor` with a `Budgets()` scan and forbids SP-13 from adding any export to `internal/obs`.

| ID | Kind | What changed | Design obligation | Risk if unnoticed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SP-13-C1 | contract-changed *(contract-only)* | The Mission repoints the observable from `contract.History.MCPInitialized` in `state/contract.json` to `contract.SessionHistory.MCPInitialized` at `contract.HistoryPath` | 00-ARCH §12.1 `mcp.server_registered` | The old text writes a `SessionHistory` document over the Monitor's own mode/reason/results file, corrupting both schemas on first write while the assertion reports `initialize-not-received` forever |
| SP-13-C2 | contract-changed *(contract-only)* | The consumed `daemon.Services` seam retyped from `func() bool` to `func(ctx context.Context) bool` | 00-ARCH §12.1 producer declaration | The pre-fix signature does not compile against develop, so `InstallMCPOp`'s `Bind` closure fails to build and commit 6 cannot land |
| SP-13-C3 | contract-changed *(contract-only)* | The consumed `internal/contract` block replaced: `History` → `SessionHistory`, `HistoryPath` added, `LoadHistory`/`SaveHistory` become pointer-based with a `path` parameter | 00-ARCH §5.19; §12.1 | The pre-fix value-semantics signatures do not exist on develop — a value-copied history losing the read-modify-write, or no compile |
| SP-13-C4 | contract-changed *(contract-only)* | §9 corrects the assertion severity `SevWarn` → `SevInfo` and adds a new instruction that SP-13 must not strengthen it | 00-ARCH §12.1; §12.3 degradation doctrine | An implementer believing `SevWarn` and "fixing" `standard.go` breaks `standard_test.go` and makes a missing MCP handshake degrade real sessions — a §12.3 violation |
| SP-13-C6 | contract-changed *(contract-only)* | The `kind == "initialized"` handler rewritten to use `contract.HistoryPath` and pointer semantics instead of a hand-built path to `state/contract.json` | 00-ARCH §12.1; §5.19 | The old instruction carried a false claim ("the same path SP-05's `session.start` path uses") that leads straight to corrupting the Monitor's state file |

Had the one-line text stood, every SP-13 handler runs against a nil Store and Ledger, `qompack mcp` answers `available:false` to everything, and SP-12's L3 stays disabled. As written, the risk is estimation: the subplan now carries store/DAG/ledger lifecycle its LOC estimate and four-subagent partition were sized before |

Violating the ordering means `DeclareProducers` never declares `CMCPRegistered` and the assertion reports not-yet-implemented on a fully working build |

| SP-13-C16 | exit-criteria-changed | The Done checklist's out-of-scope discipline widens from "**one** added line" to the whole resident-set block, error paths and two `defer Close`s, with an "and nothing else in that file" fence | §7.2 L6 / §8.7; wave-3 merge discipline | This is the list `git diff --stat develop` is reviewed against: the old wording rejects a correct implementation as out-of-scope, or hides an entire store/DAG/ledger lifecycle added to another subplan's file |

All 17 SP-13 records were ruled `designConflict: none`. Two rulings are worth keeping: C7's widening is unopposed — nothing in Qompack.md or `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` assigns the composition root's resident set to another subplan, and §3.2:408 lists `daemon, cli, commands, testutil, cmd/qompack` as composition roots that "may import anything". C8's "SP-13 merges last in wave 3" rests on `plans/README.md`'s stated wave-3 order, which README itself qualifies: "**The wave 2–5 orders above are stated conventions, not enforced ones**".

Cosmetic/correction-only: 4 records (SP-13-C5, C10, C12, C17) — three context-parameter retypes of the `MCPInitialized` seam in a Bind closure and two test rows, plus `fe68485`'s recommended-model header.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- The §11 bootstrap code block also renames `metrics` → `reg` and changes `log.Loud(…, "err", err)` to `err.Error()`, both tracking shipped develop conventions. C13 reported only the `o` → `opts` rename from the commit-plan hunk.
- The widened bootstrap makes SP-13 call seven package symbols the plan's own Interface contract still says it does not call, and `73dbd4e` did not extend that block. The Consumes preamble is unchanged: "SP-13 calls no method outside these lists" — yet the block contains no `store.Open`, no `negknow.Open`, no `symbols.New`, no `paths.Of` and no `dag` block at all, and neither interface list carries the `Close()` the two new `defer`s call. The code is buildable; the gap is that the Done checklist's type-consistency item will be checked against a contract block that never grew with the scope.
- The corrected `internal/contract` block still labels itself "§5.19" under a header claiming exact signatures from `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5, but §5.19 declares none of `SessionHistory`, `HistoryPath`, `LoadHistory` or `SaveHistory` and was never amended. The defect is perpetuated rather than introduced, but the fix commit is where it would have been caught, and ARCH-2's route is an `arch/<short-reason>` amendment landing on develop first. Related: `contract.History` was not "invented" — it exists on develop as the interface `Env.History` names; what was invented was a *struct* `History` carrying an `MCPInitialized` field.
- Commit 6 gained a whole degrade-path block but gained no test for it: the implementation bullet now demands "each failure logged `Loud` and degraded", while the bullet immediately above it — the failing-tests list — is unchanged, and no test-plan row covers `store.Open`/`dag.Open`/`negknow.Open` failing, the `opts.Store == nil` ledger skip, or the two `defer Close`s. Against `plans/README.md`'s TDD rule and §13 invariant 10, the newly-owned degradation paths are specified and untested.
- The sizing the widened scope invalidates was left verbatim: `:1293` still reads "This subplan is heavy (~2 600 LOC of implementation plus ~2 200 LOC of tests). Partition it across four parallel subagents." `73dbd4e` changed no number anywhere in the file.

### V4-VERIFY-checkpoint-rehydrate-schedule-retrieval.md

Rewrite commit `2504696` — *docs(v4-verify): correct the rows that would fail on a healthy tree* — 56 insertions, 25 deletions. Total drift `d361f06..HEAD` is 60/25; `fe68485` contributes the four-line header.

Most of the rewrite is correction verified against shipped code: the replay driver's real flags (`--out`, `--max-cpu`, `--baseline`; no `--to`, no `--json`, exit 2 on a bare `--`), `contract.HistoryPath` rather than `state/contract.json` in three places, `eval.DefaultLatencyModel()`'s 3000 ms + 150 ms/1K coefficients replacing a 0.12 ms/token model that never shipped, `MetricsOf` being a flat map so the §3.1 jq paths had been returning null, and `internal/sketch/doc.go:101` poisoning a tree-wide `grep "12\.5"`. Four edits genuinely move scope: the closing-note-3 story is inverted so that recording "the analyzer does not exist" is now forbidden rather than required, V4-SP12-10 gains a brand-new test the verifier must author in `internal/analyzer`, SP-12's idle-task priority band moves 10–60 → 110–160, and a seventh reported-only budget row B-G is added to §5.1, §3.6 invariant 9, the §5 preamble and the §8 report template. V4-SP01-04 also stops naming a `TestMainIsDispatchOnly` that exists nowhere — a `-run` pattern matching nothing exits 0, so the row was measuring nothing.

All 24 V4-VERIFY records were ruled `designConflict: none`, and three of those rulings correct the plan's own attribution: C10, C15 and C20 all cite "Qompack.md §12.1" for the contract monitor, but Qompack.md has no §12.1 — its §12 at line 1172 is the risk register, and the contract-monitor section is `00-ARCHITECTURE.md`'s. The B-G rows (C21, C22) were checked specifically for a §2.4 conflict and cleared: B-G is declared reported-only and structurally ungatable, so it adds no gate to §2.4's normative set.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 5 records (V4V-C1, C3, C8, C20, C24) — the recommended-model header (which the extractor filed as contract-only; the same commit added an identically shaped header to 21 other documents in one pass), the PowerShell line-count recipe that enables C5, the `--to` → `--baseline` flag fix, the third occurrence of the `state/history.json` repointing, and the B-G row in the §8 report template.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- V4-SP12-19's rewritten cell fixed the priority numbers and left the count assertion the same rationale invalidates: it still opens "exactly six tasks in priority order … at priorities 110…160" while `V4-SP-12-scheduler-l3.md:2729` — the document `2504696` says it harmonised against — rules the opposite verbatim: "`d.Idle().Registered()` returns nine names … Asserting "exactly six" would only pass against a bare controller — a test that cannot see the thing it checks". This file's own V4-SP05-08 already enumerates eleven registered names, and the §8 report row has no cell for the priority band the commit added. Scope-changing: a verifier asserting "exactly six" against a real `daemon.New` controller records a FAIL on correct code — the exact failure mode the commit message says it exists to remove.
- The §0 and V4-SP01-12 rewrites were not propagated into the two places that still ask for the retired framing: `:1179` still reads "submodular still inert (record the operative reason)" and the §8 report template at `:1453` still has an "operative reason:" cell. "The operative reason" is precisely the phrasing the new §0 bullet repudiates, and neither surviving cell has a slot for the two gate readings the new rows demand.
- Inside the V4-SP01-04 replacement, a third assertion neither C5 nor C6 reported: "no package-level `init` beyond `var` declarations". Satisfied today, but a new checkpoint obligation on the file's shape, not a restatement.
- C9 and C23 both describe the `--max-wall 3m` → `--max-cpu 3m` swap without noticing that 3 m is *looser* than the driver's own shipped `defaultMaxCPU = 2 * time.Minute`. Both the §2.2 gate row and the §6.3 guardrail block therefore raise the CPU ceiling 50% above default while wall falls back to 15 m — neither bound the old line carried is enforced. The sibling `V3-VERIFY…:251` runs the same driver at `--max-cpu 2m`.
- V4-SP01-06 (untouched by the diff) still runs a `-run` pattern naming `TestBudgetsMatchArchitectureTable` and `TestCheckBudgetsNeverGatesBD` over `./internal/obs/`. Neither exists; the shipped names are `TestCheckBudgets_NeverReportsBG` and `TestCheckBudgets_NeverReportsUngatedBudgets`. This is the identical matches-nothing silent pass rule 8's own citation was built to prevent, and it survives only because `tools/devtool/planchecks.go` excludes V4 documents until every wave-3 subplan appears in `landedSubplans`. A commit that added a B-G row with two verified test names left the neighbouring obs row naming two that do not exist.

## Wave 4 (group V5) — SP-14, SP-15, SP-16, V5-VERIFY

Four files, 106 verified change records, 50 of them scope-changing. Every file was rewritten by exactly one commit inside merge c0c5627, except V5-VERIFY, which also carries a one-line fix from 988bc08. The wave's common thread: three of the four rewrites are corrections toward wave-1 code that had already shipped a seam the plan proposed to invent.

### V5-SP-14-slash-commands-and-observability.md

Rewritten by `b1ce3b2` — *docs(v5-sp-14): give status its own op instead of replacing SP-05's* — `1 file changed, 236 insertions(+), 109 deletions(-)`. (`fe68485` separately added the 4-line recommended-model header; `d361f06..HEAD` totals 240/109.)

The rewrite is built around one discovery: SP-14 was specified to register a handler for `ipc.Op("status")`, and `Options` registrations win over `defaultRoutes` (`internal/daemon/options_test.go:51`), so that registration would have silently displaced SP-05's shipped `d.handleStatus` (`internal/daemon/daemon.go:319`) and left `test/bench/hotpath/measure.go` unable to unmarshal `daemon.StatusSnapshot`. The fix gives SP-14 its own `ipc.Op("status.full")` op, a new `StatusFull` type and `CollectFull` collector, and a two-request fetch protocol with per-section fallback — which in turn forces a package-private JSON mirror of the daemon payload plus a pinned fixture. A second cluster retires the invented `pluginmanifest.CommandSpec`/`CommandMarkdown`/`CommandFiles` API in favour of extending the shipped `CommandDoc` (`internal/pluginmanifest/manifest.go:83`) and `renderCommand`, which makes regenerating the seven committed `plugin/commands/*.md` an obligation and drags in a declared amendment to 00-ARCHITECTURE §2.3. A third widens the `cli` work from "one switch arm" to three data edits — including an `errUsage` sentinel inside SP-01's `dispatch.go` — because there is no subcommand switch in package `cli` at all; `Dispatch` matches argv against the `All()` data table. A fourth adds B-G as a seventh rendered latency budget sourced from `obs.Budgets()` rather than from local constants.

**Design conflict (SP-14-C4).** The new mirror, its committed fixture and its drift test rest on a misreading of 00-ARCHITECTURE §3.2. Line 408 reads verbatim: "`daemon`, `cli`, `commands`, `testutil`, `cmd/qompack`, and every `test/**` harness … | **composition roots** — may import anything; nothing may import them". `commands` is on that row. The plan invokes exactly this reading two sentences earlier to justify `cli → commands` ("*may import anything* covers the other members of that row") and then invokes the opposite reading to forbid `commands → daemon`: "It is a mirror rather than an import because §3.2 forbids importing internal/daemon". The permissive reading is what ships — `tools/devtool/importgraph.go:179-180` is `if !c.recognized || c.isRoot { continue // composition roots may import anything }`, so `commands`' imports are never checked. A duplicated schema and a hand-maintained fixture are a real cost taken on for a rule that does not exist.

**Design conflict (SP-14-C13) — corrective direction.** The *pre-rewrite* text contradicted the architecture, and the rewrite removes it. The deleted sentence read: "`checkpointFinalizeBudget` and `budgetMCPCallMs` restate 00-ARCHITECTURE §2.4 budgets that are *not* config keys (only `runtime.hotPath.budgetMs` is), so they are legitimately constants here." 00-ARCHITECTURE Appendix C lines 2361-2364 say otherwise, verbatim: `"budgets": { "l0IngestMs": 2, "l0ProcessMs": 50,      // B-B, B-C` / `"checkpointFinalizeMs": 2000,            // B-E` / `"mcpToolCallMs": 250,                    // B-F` / `"hookDegradedMs": 1000 },                // B-G (§12.3 spool append)`. The old plan text was false against the normative spec, not merely against the shipped code.

**Design conflict (SP-14-C14 / SP-14-C20).** 00-ARCHITECTURE §5.17 is normative and states the surface per-hook. Line 1716 reads verbatim: "hook latency p50/p99 per hook against budgets B-A/B-D, frontier turn and residual tokens, last". The rewrite concedes that on the daemon path — the path the plan says `status` prefers, "because `obs.Registry` is daemon-resident, so the latency section is *only* real on this path" — those rows render `no samples`, because `internal/daemon/handlers.go:753-760` fills its latency map from `obs.Budgets()` plus `hook_controlled_observed` and nothing else. No remedy is proposed. Compounding it, C20 rewrote the DoD bullet that used to restate §5.17's wording — "per-hook latency percentiles against B-A and B-D" — into "latency percentiles for every entry of `obs.Budgets()`", dropping "per hook". The exit criteria and the normative spec now disagree about what shipping `/qompack:status` means.

**Design conflict (SP-14-C8) — tension, not contradiction.** 00-ARCHITECTURE §0 lines 16-18/24-26 scope the amendment rule to §5 only ("It may not change an interface in §5 without an amendment commit … You open a branch `arch/<short-reason>` off `develop`, change §5, get it merged, and rebase"), so a §2.3 edit needs no `arch/` branch by the letter of the rule, and the plan says so correctly. But §9 line 2192 reads "Emergency architecture changes: `arch/<reason>` off `develop`, must land before dependent work", and the plan schedules "as a one-row, one-line edit to `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §2.3, made in commit 7" of a `feat/sp14-*` branch that merges last in wave 4 (plans/README.md:45, "Wave 4: SP-15 → SP-16 → SP-14"), with no mechanism named for SP-15 or SP-16 touching the same block. The verifier also found the edit target does not exist as described: §2.3 is a fenced ASCII tree, not a markdown table, and every non-hook subcommand shares one line.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 5 records (SP-14-C23, SP-14-C24, SP-14-C25, SP-14-C26, SP-14-C27) — prose alignment with the substantive changes plus `fe68485`'s recommended-model header; the two interesting ones correct factual claims about shipped code (`ipc.HotPathMode` has no `String()` method; `dispatchOp` stamps `Mode` and `Hot` on every response, so the handler must not).

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- The "What exists when you finish" paragraph — the plan's one-paragraph statement of deliverables — was rewritten to restate both headline scope changes (`ipc.OpStatus` untouched + `status.full`; `commandSpecs` extended in place), and no record covered it.
- Global rule 8's error-routing contract changed (hunk `@@ -341,7 +368,7 @@`), flagged in the plan as "normative, because every golden depends on it": `RunSlash` prints nothing → `runSlash` tags `errAlreadyReported` and `errUsage`. It splits exit-code policy across two packages and adds a second dependency on SP-01's package-private `errAlreadyReported`.
- A new performance-budget obligation binds `CollectFull` to a p95 < 250 ms local budget ("The daemon-side `status.full` handler is likewise off the hot path — `ipc.Op.HotPath()` is true only for the three `observe.*` ops").
- Two more collect-block-5 latency rows changed that SP-14-C13's table omits, including the gated B-A row — the one row `bench-gate` hard-fails on.
- Two signature edits inside SP-14's own package (`specFor`, `newFlagSet`) propagate the `CommandSpec`→`CommandDoc` retirement into two further file specs.
- Commit 1's checklist converted from a file-creation task to a six-part in-place modification of `manifest.go` — the operative instruction where C5/C6/C7 actually land as work.
- A new normative decomposition of `Collect` pins which of the eleven numbered blocks each op owns, and is the only place the plan asserts block 5 (latency) is fully covered by the daemon's reply — the assumption C14's narrowing rests on.
- A new field-semantics rule keeping `Description` and `Summary` as two fields, which is what `TestCommandDocFieldsPreserveShippedValues` actually tests.

### V5-SP-15-analyzer-selection-and-grammar.md

Rewritten by `681cd9a` — *docs(v5-sp-15): resolve the ship-order gate against the shipped config* — `1 file changed, 355 insertions(+), 118 deletions(-)`. (With `fe68485`'s header, `d361f06..HEAD` is 359/118.)

This is the wave's largest rewrite and the only one that reverses a decision rather than correcting a fact. The plan previously recorded a prohibition — "Decision — there is no `selection.submodular.enabled` key … Do **not** add the key if it is absent" — and now records the opposite: the key exists on `develop` (`internal/config/runtime.go:98`, defaulted false at `defaults.go:157`) and SP-15 flips its default to `true`. That reversal pulls in four new file edits inside SP-01-owned packages, including inverting a shipped build-order guard's assertion, and takes the ownership criterion from "exactly two files modified outside packages SP-15 owns" to "exactly six". Separately the rewrite strips SP-15's ownership of the `FormatWarning` wording and re-points every warning golden at SP-01's shipped `[qompack] possible loop:` text; changes `AttachThrashWarning` from `Daemon` to `*Options` because the shipped route table is frozen by `New` and read unguarded on the B-A path; swaps the two constructor guards' order to match `internal/analyzer/selector.go`'s normative doc comment; and re-parents both sentinels onto `core.ErrBudget`/`core.ErrNotImplemented`. Most of it is correct reconciliation against wave-1 code. The exception is the §6.5 narrowing: the selector now ships "measured, not wired", with its production consumer deferred to SP-16 and to a "wave-5 rehydration slice" that no plan document defines.

**Design conflict (SP-15-C1 / SP-15-C25).** plans/TRACEABILITY.md:61 reads verbatim: "| 5 — Selection | DAG (built in SP-07), thin slicing, Δ-scoring, suffix-constrained submodular | SP-07 (DAG/slicing) + SP-15 | V5 |". It names exactly two owners and no third. SP-15's new Mission paragraph declares "in wave 4 the selector ships measured, not wired. Its only callers are the two replay policies in `test/replay` … no production code path constructs a `Selector`", and its design-coverage checklist now records Qompack.md:590 — "This should allocate the post-compact budget instead of \"top 5 files, 5K each.\" Slice membership and Δ-scores become the coverage weights." — as "delivered as a measured policy only; the production allocator is deferred". TRACEABILITY asserts full coverage of Phase 5 where the plan records partial coverage.

**Design conflict (SP-15-C20).** The scope note prepended to the Measurable Definition of Done is binding on every criterion below it and declares Phase 5's exit number "a **replay-harness result**, not a live-path measurement … A reviewer must not read the criteria below as claiming otherwise." Qompack.md:590 states the obligation as production allocation, and TRACEABILITY.md:61 gates Phase 5 at V5. A criterion satisfied by two test-only policies scoring differently on a synthetic corpus cannot discharge that sentence, so V5 can close Phase 5 while the design text remains unimplemented.

**Design conflict (SP-15-C2).** plans/TRACEABILITY.md:22 reads verbatim: "| G3.3 | Eager restoration | SP-11 | Pointer-first, 8–12K budget; refined by SP-16 promotion |". The new Out-of-scope row defers "the rehydrator's 8–12K allocation, and `checkpoint.Truncate`'s pointer ordering" to "**SP-16** (pointer ordering / `OrderPointers`) and the **wave-5 rehydration slice** (allocation)". plans/README.md:46 gives "Wave 5: SP-17 → SP-18"; SP-11 is wave 3 and merges *before* SP-15. The row therefore contradicts both TRACEABILITY's G3.3 ownership and README's wave-5 membership, and names an owner no plan document defines.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 3 records (SP-15-C19, SP-15-C31, SP-15-C32) — a `warn_test.go` checklist miscount the rewrite introduced (says 11 tests, the table holds 10), an annotation making C9's ownership move legible in the contract section, and `fe68485`'s header.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- **SP-15 is now desynchronised from its own gating checkpoint.** C12 re-points every SP-15 golden to `[qompack] possible loop:`, but V5-VERIFY still requires the old string in two places: row I-15.15 at line 430 ("`\"[qompack] thrash:\"` and `\"repeated 11×\"` present") and the hand-run scenario at lines 772-773. Shipped `internal/grammar/formatwarning.go:22` emits `possible loop:`. That this is oversight rather than decision is shown by I-15.13 at line 428, which *was* reconciled to the rewrite in the same audit pass.
- `PromptAddendum`'s output contract changed — from "the warnings joined with `\n`" to "`FormatWarning` applied to each warning, joined with `\n`" — the third member of the C10/C11 triple, and the one on the user-visible hook path.
- A test row was deleted outright, not renumbered: `TestFormatWarningNoTurns` (asserting `"(turns unknown)"`) disappears; the obligation moves from testing the formatter to testing its caller.
- The pre-p error's format string changed on both operand and text (`%q` → `%s`, plus the word "precedes") inside the C8 hunk, uncredited — it is what makes the plan's message match shipped `selector.go:47`.
- Section 7's declared edit size changed from one line to four, in both the heading and the body — the number C22 and C26 both quote back.
- Commit 2 gained a fourth enforcement of the `FormatWarning` freeze: `git diff --exit-code develop -- internal/grammar/formatwarning.go …` embedded in the commit checklist.

### V5-SP-16-phase7-refinements.md

Rewritten by `229906f` — *docs(v5-sp-16): use SP-06's reserved record instead of a second index* — `1 file changed, 145 insertions(+), 40 deletions(-)`. (With `fe68485`'s header, `d361f06..HEAD` is 149/40.)

Two corrections to wave-1 reality, each propagated through the whole document. First, SP-01's `SkiRentalShouldWrite` is not a false stub: `internal/scheduler/formulas.go:32-38` ships the real closed form with two committed tests, so commit 2's obligation changes from "delete the stub" to "move the real closed form out of `formulas.go`, route it through the new threshold helper, and keep SP-01's two tests green unedited as the proof" — which adds an SP-01-owned file as an edit site. Second, the planned `.qompack/sketches/segments/INDEX.jsonl` sidecar is deleted entirely and the durable bloom lookup routed through SP-06's already-shipped `segBloomRec` / `segOpBloom` seam in `index/segments.jsonl` (`internal/store/segments.go:22, :62-70, :157-163`). That substitution is not free: it adds an unexported `recordBloomRef`, changes SP-06's `openSegLog` signature to take `root` and `cfg`, adds two `segLog` fields, and drags `internal/store/open.go` and `internal/store/segments_test.go` in as edit sites — taking the anchored-edit inventory from "seven sites across six files" to "fourteen sites across twelve files". The rewrite also inverts the idle-task priorities after reading the shipped `IdleController`'s "Lower prio runs first" semantics, and adds an exit criterion holding the warm-started Count-Min to Appendix A's accuracy bound rather than only to "never under-count".
