**Design conflict (SP-16-C1 / SP-16-C3 / SP-16-C5) — phantom citation.** The rewrite's new text repeatedly grounds itself on a section that does not exist: "because 00-ARCHITECTURE §14.1 requires fully specified pure functions to be implemented" (plans/V5-SP-16-phase7-refinements.md:17), and again in C3's inserted paragraph, "under 00-ARCHITECTURE §14.1's 'fully specified pure functions are implemented, not stubbed' rule". 00-ARCHITECTURE.md has no §14.1 — line 2501 is "## 14. Wave and subplan map", a wave table and nothing else. The rule lives at plans/V1-SP-01-foundation-toolchain-and-contracts.md:1393: "3. **Pure functions that are fully specified by the architecture are implemented, not stubbed.** These are: … `scheduler.SkiRentalShouldWrite` …". The mis-citation is pre-existing convention (`internal/chunk/params.go:57` makes the same one), so the rewrite propagated it rather than invented it — but three of this file's corrections now rest on a pointer that resolves to nothing.

**Design conflict (SP-16-C5) — TDD carve-out.** plans/README.md:59 states the rule verbatim: "**TDD:** within each commit, tests are written and observed failing before implementation, then observed passing." The rewritten commit-2 bullet carves six rows out of it, saying they "are **green from the first run** … regression coverage for commit 2's move, not red-first TDD", without citing an exception. It is defensible — the code they cover shipped in wave 0 — but the plan states the carve-out as fact rather than as a reconciled exception. The verifier also found the bullet self-contradicts: the same sentence says the run "**must fail to compile**" because the new symbols do not exist, and those six rows live in `internal/scheduler/skirental_test.go`, the very file stated not to compile, so no test in the package runs at all.

Cosmetic/correction-only: 4 records (SP-16-C2, SP-16-C23, SP-16-C24, SP-16-C25) — the Consumes-block comment corrected the same way as C1, a `BuildSegmentBloom` doc comment that stopped claiming `segLog` already holds a root and config, SP-01 added to the subagent-strategy list of subplans whose files this branch anchors into, and `fe68485`'s header. (SP-16-C21's design obligation was ADJUSTED: the extractor cited Qompack.md §12.3, which has no numbered subsections; the plan's own bare "§12.3" correctly resolves to 00-ARCHITECTURE.md:2461.)

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- A five-line doc-comment obligation added *inside* the `skirental.go` code block binds a specific quoted sentence out of SP-01's shipped comment ("neither that ratio nor its operands may ever appear as a literal in this package") to the new file — distinct from C3's prose and C4's checklist line, and what keeps the nomagic rationale attached to the code the lint guards.
- A new interface-downcast contract: `BackfillSegmentBlooms` reaches `recordBloomRef` with `sl, ok := s.Segments().(*segLog)`, type-asserting §5.8's `SegmentLog` interface down to the unexported concrete type and defining a silent-degrade path for every non-`FSStore` implementation.
- A new locking constraint forking the write path in two: `Close` already holds `l.mu`, so it must call `l.append` directly rather than `recordBloomRef`, which takes the lock itself. The record now has two distinct producers separated only by a deadlock rule.
- A new disabled-config byte-identity contract: `BuildSegmentBloom` returns `("", 0, nil)` when the feature is off, so `segments.jsonl` stays byte-identical to its pre-SP-16 shape — the clause that keeps the change backward-compatible.
- The pre-audit anchored-edit inventory was already arithmetically wrong: the header said "seven sites across six files" over a table whose rows sum to 9 edits across 9 files. The true widening is 9 → 14 sites and 9 → 12 files, not 7 → 14 and 6 → 12.

### V5-VERIFY-commands-selection-grammar-and-refinements.md

Rewritten by `dafc5bf` — *docs(v5-verify): fix the bench flags and the patterns that match nothing* — `1 file changed, 52 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)`; plus `988bc08` — *docs(plans): fix the unsatisfiable commands runpatterns found* — `1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)` on this file. (`d361f06..HEAD` totals 57/15 including `fe68485`'s header.)

The smallest drift in the wave and the least cosmetic. `dafc5bf` does four scope-changing things: it adds a whole new budget (B-G) and a whole new inventory row (I-16.17) that V5 must measure and close; it makes V5 the owner of an uncommitted code change to SP-05's `test/bench/hotpath` harness, without which budget B-A′ is unmeasurable at all; it rewrites two sign-off criteria (zero `t.Skip`, zero `ErrNotImplemented`) from absolute zeroes into named-survivor allowances; and it converts I-15.13's invariant-4 check from a `"Pos <"` string grep into an AST comparison. Two rows fix commands that as written could never fail — I-01.18's `t.Skip` sweep, whose old substring pattern matched 113 lines repo-wide, and (via `988bc08`) §3.8's Phase-7 procedure, whose backslash-escaped `\|` inside a non-table `-run` pattern RE2 reads as a literal pipe, so it matched nothing and exited 0. The rewrite left one internal incoherence: the §8.2 report template at line 1131 still says "histograms & six budgets" while I-01.10 at line 116 now demands seven.

No record in this file carries a design conflict against Qompack.md, 00-ARCHITECTURE.md or TRACEABILITY.md — every `designConflict` verdict is "none", and several are explicitly reinforcing (B-G is ungated and §2.4 is scoped to "what CI gates on"; refusing a second bloom index reinforces §13 invariant 3). The conflicts this file creates are internal and cross-document: the §8.2 template versus I-01.10 (V5V-C2), and the `[qompack] thrash:` rows versus SP-15's rewrite (recorded under SP-15-C12).

Cosmetic/correction-only: 1 record (V5V-C1) — `fe68485`'s recommended-model header.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- **I-01.18's rewritten cell is under-scoped by roughly a factor of twenty.** It asserts that `internal/tokens/tokenstest/estimator_suite.go` "is the one hit this row exists to catch". Against the shipped tree, `grep -rEl '^\s*t\.Skip(f|Now)?\(' --include=*.go internal/ | grep 'test/'` returns 21 files, every one a `<pkg>test` conformance suite with a structurally identical helper — `canontest/suite.go:96-103`, `analyzertest/suite.go:201-208`, `storetest`, `dagtest`, `chunktest`, `ipctest`, `contracttest`. `canon` is SP-04, merged in wave 1, and its `t.Skip(ruleW1SkipMsg)` is still in source. I-01.18 as rewritten fails at V5 on all of them while naming remediation for one.
- **I-01.17's new cell misattributes the owing wave, and one of the seven deletions is unowned.** It says "wave 4 delivers all seven", but `mcp` is SP-13's and SP-13 is wave 3; SP-14's own plan removes six rows and says verbatim "(leaving `mcp`, `fsck`, `doctor` and `bench` to their owners)", while V4-SP-13 never mentions `internal/cli/commands.go` or `notImplemented` at all. Exactly one of the seven has no owning checklist.
- The §8.2 template's I-01.18 row (line 1139) is stale in the same way I-01.10's is: it still reads "zero `t.Skip` in conformance suites … count:" while the criterion no longer counts raw hits.
- The corrected B-E command keeps `--iterations 200`, which is consumed only for B-A/B-D; because the harness measures every budget on every run, that command also emits a full B-A row from 200 spawns into `v5-be.json`, against B-A's own command specifying `--iterations 5000`. Two artifacts from the same §5 run carry contradictory B-A p99s at a 25× sample-size difference, and nothing says which to record.

## Wave 5 (group V6) — SP-17, SP-18, V6-VERIFY

Three files, 80 verified change records, 43 of them scope-changing and 24 contract-only. Two records carry a design conflict, both in SP-18, and both are cases where the plan is right about the shipped tree and `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` is the unamended party. The wave's common thread is deflation: SP-18 stops planning to write four artifacts that already exist, SP-17 removes a second version source and a placeholder premise, and V6-VERIFY recounts the doc set, the CI jobs and the §12.3 rows against what is actually there.

### V6-SP-17-packaging-hardening-and-release.md

Rewritten by `92fd903` *docs(v6-sp-17): remove the second version source and the placeholder premise* (235 insertions, 98 deletions), plus `0fff22c` *fix(replay): refuse a cross-tier baseline and a missing one* (1/1) and `fe68485` *docs(verify): merge every wave-1 session's findings into V2-VERIFY* (4/0). Total drift from d361f06: **239 insertions(+), 98 deletions(-)**.

The rewrite deletes a produced symbol and rebuilds everything that pointed at it: `pluginmanifest.DefaultVersion` — declared in the old plan as "the single source of truth for the plugin version" — is removed from Produces, and the goreleaser `-X` flags, `set-version`, `verify-version`, the bundle assembler, `doctor`'s `binary` check, PV-1 and the Done checklist are all repointed at the already-shipped `internal/core.Version` (`internal/core/version.go`, `var Version = "0.1.0"`). Two start-state premises were false and both carried work with them: `.github/workflows/release.yml` is a fully wired tag-triggered job (`.github/workflows/release.yml:1-33`), not a placeholder, so "replace" became "extend, preserving `fetch-depth: 0`, the pinned toolchain, the `ci-local` gate and the three permission grants" plus a regression test; and the `fsck`/`doctor` stubs exit non-zero (`internal/cli/commands.go:49-62`), which is what §7's `--strict` exit policy now rests on. Scope then widened in four places the plan had never carried: a `--bundle native|universal|source` selector on `test/bench/hotpath` (new §14, which V6-VERIFY already depended on at d361f06), the `test/replay --live` / `SetLiveRunner` wiring for §6.3 tier 3 (new §15, assigned to SP-17 by the later architecture correction `ca63567`), `fsck --gc-all`, and check 3's `--repair` root retirement that discharges SP-06's D15 residual. Smaller but behaviour-visible: the release trigger narrows from `v*.*.*` to `v[1-9]*.*.*` so wave verification tags cannot start a release run, and the redaction test plan is rebuilt on the ten rule names actually shipped in `internal/redact/rules.go:83-131`.

Verifier corrections worth naming. **SP-17-C8** was extracted as scope-changing and downgraded to contract-only: DoD item 13's snapshot dry run already existed at d361f06, so the `IsSnapshot` gate fixes the mechanism that made an existing obligation unreachable rather than adding one — the extractor overstated it. **SP-17-C11** was extracted as `scope-narrowed` and re-attributed to `correction-only (start-state premise corrected)`; its scope-changing materiality stands, because the corrected premise carries a preservation obligation and a regression test.

Two near-misses the verifier flagged inside otherwise-clean rulings, neither rising to a conflict. On **SP-17-C13**, ARCH §9:2196 reads "**Tags.** `v0.<wave>.<n>` on `develop` after each verification; `v<semver>` on `main` at release" — a `v0.x.y` *release* semver would be excluded by the new `v[1-9]*.*.*` filter, but §9 constrains tag naming, not the workflow trigger. On **SP-17-C16**, Qompack.md §7.4:723 reads `├── objects/                       # content-addressed, zstd-compressed`, which the new bare-name branch does not literally match; the divergence originates in SP-06's shipped `store.compression: "none"` option (`internal/store/objects.go:18-25`), so the rewrite makes the plan match shipped code rather than creating the gap.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 3 records (SP-17-C27, C31, C32) — the fsck/doctor stub exit-code premise inverted from 0 to non-zero, the nightly job renamed `replay-live` → `replay-recorded` to match the post-audit fix round, and the recommended-model header block.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- The **third start-state fact** and the obligation it carries: "`qompack version` also already exists and prints `core.Version`; §6 below extends it with `BuildInfo` and `--json` rather than introducing it", paired with a commit-1 checklist rewrite from "Add `internal/cli/version.go` … and dispatch `qompack version`" to "point the **existing** `version` command's `Run` at `RunVersion`; do not register a second one". Confirmed at `internal/cli/commands.go:64-67`.
- `BuildInfo.Manifest` was **redefined, not repointed**: `Manifest: pluginmanifest.DefaultVersion,` → `Manifest: pluginmanifest.Default(core.Version).Plugin.Version,`, with a new comment making the field "the tripwire: if a second version source is ever introduced, this is the first place the two disagree in public". `TestVersionStamping` moved from six exact fields to seven.
- **PV-1 was rewritten, not just repointed**, and carries no claim id: its subject is now explicitly the *committed* `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and its failure mode is restated as "a `set-version` run edited `internal/core/version.go` but did not regenerate `plugin/**`".
- §5 step 3's generator call changed shape — `pluginmanifest.Files()` → `pluginmanifest.Default(version).Files()` — which is what makes step 3's own version assertion satisfiable at all.
- §1's opening flipped from "Replaces SP-01's skeleton" to "Extends SP-01's working skeleton", and named the action-pin delta (`~> v2` → `~> v2.6`; the pin lives in the workflow, `.goreleaser.yaml` carries none).
- Subagent A's exclusive file set lost `internal/pluginmanifest/version.go` — the parallel-execution half of C1. Under the old plan a subagent held write ownership of a file in SP-01's package.

### V6-SP-18-documentation-and-uat.md

Rewritten by `d2e9cd5` *docs(v6-sp-18): stop planning to write four artifacts that already exist* (187 insertions, 104 deletions), plus `fe68485` (4/0). Total drift from d361f06: **191 insertions(+), 104 deletions(-)**.

The rewrite's purpose is its subject line: four artifacts the plan proposed to create already exist on disk, and the plan is re-cut against them. `docs/config-reference.md` is already shipped and complete (`tools/devtool/genconfigdocs.go`, a working 257-line generator), `docs/adr/` already holds four low-numbered files, the upstream-tracker form is already populated, and `docs/mcp-tools.md` already exists — so §3 changes from "replace the task body" to a five-row preservation table around `--check`, `normalizeNewlines` and the flag set; the twelve architecture ADRs are renumbered `0001`–`0012` → `0200`–`0211` to stop overwriting committed decision records; and commit 5 changes from filling a stub to rewriting a populated form. Two ownership moves follow the sibling rewrite: `docs/security.md` and its entire §13 spec, its eight required headings and its three-test `test/docs/security_test.go` are struck from SP-18 and handed to SP-17 (`OwnedDocs()` 22 → 21), as are `docs/release.md` and `docs/install.md`. The frozen "72 leaves / 72 `Meta` entries" contract is deleted and replaced with a walk-derived count the plan explicitly refuses to freeze (88 on the authoring branch); the `runtime.*` enumeration grows 22 → 39 keys with `budgets`, `selection` and `tokens`; and `selection.submodular.enabled` — a `json:"-"` field `config --set` cannot address — is relocated to `runtime.selection.submodularEnabled`. Commit 7 gains a hard mid-flight dependency: it must merge the `develop` that carries SP-17 before it can close.

Verifier correction worth naming: **SP-18-C6** was extracted as scope-changing and downgraded to contract-only — the inventory shrink and the test rename move in lockstep with the checkpoint (`plans/V6-VERIFY-production-readiness-and-uat.md:439`), so it is inventory mechanics, not a scope move. The extractor overstated it.

**Design conflict — SP-18-C10, the `docs` CI job.** ARCH §8, line 2105, is normative and unamended: "| `docs` | ubuntu | `devtool gen-config-docs`, `git diff --exit-code` — `docs/config-reference.md` can never drift from `config.Defaults()` |". SP-18's Mission item 2 now asserts the opposite: "**The `docs` CI job runs `go run ./tools/devtool gen-config-docs --check` and nothing else** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:260`). It does *not* run `git diff --exit-code`". The shipped tree agrees with SP-18 — `ci.yml`'s `docs:` job at line 254 has exactly one step, `- run: go run ./tools/devtool gen-config-docs --check` — so ARCH §8's row is the stale side. But per ARCH §0:16-26 and §9:2192 that correction belongs in an `arch/<reason>` amendment commit, and none landed (`ca63567` amended other §8 rows and left this one). The same contradiction propagates to `plans/V1-SP-01-foundation-toolchain-and-contracts.md:305`: "§8's `docs` job runs `devtool gen-config-docs --check` and `git diff --exit-code`, which requires the generated file to already be in the tree". Two normative documents now disagree with SP-18 and with the shipped workflow.

**Design conflict — SP-18-C18, the write-set prefix count.** ARCH §13 invariant 7, line 2492: "7. **No network. No telemetry. No writes outside `.qompack/`** (plus `~/.qompack/` for the global layer). CI asserts the import graph and a runtime test asserts the write set." SP-18 now instructs the shipped docs to state four: "**The write-set commitment is four prefixes, not two, and the doc says four.**" — while separately instructing `docs/architecture.md` to quote invariant 7 verbatim. One doc set therefore carries both a two-prefix invariant and a four-prefix enumeration. The plan is truthful about the tree (`internal/ipc/resolve.go:30-38` resolves `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/qompack/<hash12>.sock`, then a temp-dir fallback) and ARCH is the stale side, but no `arch/<reason>` amendment landed, and the reconciliation lives only in SP-18's prose — no test asserts it.

**Design conflict — SP-18-C18, the daemon registry.** The same added paragraph ends "there is no daemon registry file today, and the doc does not promise one". ARCH line 449 says the opposite: "Global, cross-project state lives in `~/.qompack/` (`config.json` user-global layer, `daemons.json` registry, `calibration.json`)." `grep -rn 'daemons.json' internal/ cmd/` returns nothing, so SP-18 is again right about the tree and ARCH is unamended. This is also where SP-18-C5's dropped assertion lands: the deleted `test/docs/security_test.go` was the only artifact that would have caught the divergence.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 6 records (SP-18-C9, C17, C24, C26, C27, C29) — start-state facts corrected against the shipped tree (four artifacts already exist; `docs/mcp-tools.md` added to the inventory), the ARCH §12.3 failure-row count corrected ten → nine with a nineteen-row Loud glossary, commit 1's test tally corrected 23 → 21, the recommended-model header, and one residual the rewrite did **not** clear: commit 1's mandated message subject still reads "docs(sp18): README, architecture digest, ADRs, security posture, and the docs contract harness" although that commit no longer writes `docs/security.md` (SP-18-C27, `plans/V6-SP-18-documentation-and-uat.md:1088`).

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- ARCH §5.22a's design-context anchor was **reassigned**, not merely footnoted: the heading becomes `### ARCH §5.22a — the redaction rule list (docs/uat.md UAT-12; SP-17's docs/security.md carries the inventory in full)`. UAT-12's own rewritten text then declines to name any rule — "the rule name inside the placeholder is whatever `redact.Rules()` reports — the scenario asserts redaction happened, not a particular rule name" — so after `d2e9cd5` **no SP-18 artifact names a single redaction rule**.
- The companion ARCH §13 heading lost `docs/security.md` too, so invariant 7's verbatim two-prefix text is now quoted in exactly one SP-18-owned document while the four-prefix enumeration appears in three others.
- A **new standing rule binding future waves** was added, with no test named to enforce it: "If a future wave *does* add a key under an Appendix C section, that section's note must gain its own disclaimer in the same commit — an undisclosed additive key is precisely the drift this file exists to prevent."
- The `runtime` `SectionNote` string itself was lengthened to enumerate `budgets`, `selection.submodularEnabled` and `tokens` — and `TestConfigReferenceRuntimeSectionIsMarkedAdditive` compares that sentence verbatim inside the committed generated page.
- The generated page's preamble wording changed ("regenerates it and fails on any diff" → "re-renders it and fails on any difference"), and `TestConfigReferenceNotStale` compares the page byte-for-byte, so this is load-bearing on the committed artifact, not editorial.
- §12's collision inventory undercounts the low block by six entries: it names 0008/0009/0011/0012, but re-running its own grep also returns 0015, 0016, 0017, 0018, 0100 and 0101.
- The Mission's finish-state test-file count moved eleven → ten independently of the subagent-strategy counts in SP-18-C25, so reconciling the two sentences needs both edits.

### V6-VERIFY-production-readiness-and-uat.md

Rewritten by `73b1706` *docs(v6-verify): recount the doc set, the CI jobs and the §12.3 rows* (75 insertions, 38 deletions), plus `fe68485` (4/0). Total drift from d361f06: **79 insertions(+), 38 deletions(-)**.

Most of this rewrite is a re-count and re-anchor pass driven by the two sibling plans and the shipped tree: the develop verification tag moves `v0.5.1` → `v0.5.0` to fit the V2..V5 series, the owned doc set drops 22 → 21 because `docs/security.md` is SP-17's, §12.3 drops ten → nine rows to match ARCH's actual table, the CI job count rises thirteen → fourteen because `ci.yml`'s `lint-windows` was never enumerated, SP-18's ADR block is renumbered `0200`–`0211`, and the frozen "72 leaves / 72 `Meta`" exit criterion is unfrozen to a recorded correspondence. Two corrections turn vacuous gates into real ones: §1.2.10's inventory command widens from `-run 'TestGate_'` to also cover `TestPhase0_` and `TestReplayDriver_` (three of the eight replay-gate failure modes live outside the `TestGate_` prefix and were reported PASS while never executing), and §3.2's launcher fault arm changes from deleting a `bin/<goos>-<goarch>/` directory that does not exist to deleting the single flat binary the shims actually resolve. It also adds real obligations: the B-G `hook_degraded` budget at three sites, a mutation proof that the `gen-config-docs` staleness gate can fail, an explicit-refspec push rule replacing `git push --tags`, and an entirely new §10 "Release prep" subsection that pulls SP-17's deferred `set-version` commit into V6 and pins the release semver to `v1.0.0` — the last of which is where the wave's sharpest defect sits.

**Design conflict — SP-V6-C15 and SP-V6-C14, the phantom version source.** The verifier's ruling is narrow and worth stating exactly: no conflict with Qompack.md, 00-ARCHITECTURE.md or TRACEABILITY.md — none of the three names a version symbol or a release-prep owner — but a head-on conflict with the sibling wave-5 plan produced by the same audit round. V6-VERIFY's new §10 subsection opens: "**Nothing in wave 5 bumps the version off `0.1.0`.** SP-17 ships `pluginmanifest.DefaultVersion = "0.1.0"`, a `CHANGELOG.md` whose top heading is `## [0.1.0]`, and a `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` carrying `"version": "0.1.0"`", and it instructs the operator to run `set-version` against `internal/pluginmanifest/version.go`. SP-17 states the opposite in bold: "**`pluginmanifest.DefaultVersion` does not exist and must not be created.**" (`plans/V6-SP-17-packaging-hardening-and-release.md:393`), and its DoD item 16 (`:1374`) makes the symbol's absence from the tree a merge blocker. The tree agrees with SP-17: `grep -rn 'DefaultVersion' --include=*.go .` returns nothing, `internal/pluginmanifest/` holds only `golden_test.go`, `manifest.go` and `manifest_test.go` — no `version.go` — and `internal/core/version.go:6` holds the single `var Version = "0.1.0"`. An operator following V6 §10 literally creates the second version source SP-17 removed, ships a v1.0.0 binary whose `qompack version`, daemon lock, daemon status payload and MCP handshake all still say 0.1.0 while every version assertion passes, and fails SP-17's DoD 16 on the very commit V6 makes mandatory. The same symbol is propagated into the §8.1 signed completion report (SP-V6-C14). A secondary note from the same ruling: V6 now writes production source (`internal/core/version.go`, `plugin/**`, `CHANGELOG.md`) on `verify/v6`, which ARCH §9:2188 permits — "Fixes for it go on `verify/v<K>` cut from `develop`" — but which no prior checkpoint did.

Verifier corrections worth naming. **SP-V6-C3** was extracted as `scope-narrowed` / scope-changing and downgraded to `correction-only` / cosmetic: dropping §1.17.14 from ten driven-failure tests to nine moves the plan *into* agreement with ARCH §12.3's nine-row table, so it is a correction, not a narrowing — the extractor overstated it. The verifier kept the residual attached to it: the reassigned `sync → spool` row's *recovery-paragraph* obligation now has no owner in §1 or §2, even though §1.18.7 still requires the Loud glossary to carry the §12.2 transition. **SP-V6-C4, C6, C9 and C12** were adjusted on obligation attribution and risk; their kind and materiality stand as extracted.

Cosmetic / correction-only: 4 records (SP-V6-C3, C10, C18, C19) — the §12.3 row recount, the §4.2 `docs`-job row corrected to `gen-config-docs --check`, a wrong provenance sha for `lint-windows` (the new paragraph cites `e943b23`, which only added a `timeout-minutes`; the job was added by `1e48214`), and the recommended-model header.

**Caught by the verifier, not the extractor**

- §2.18 item 9 gained a **cross-subplan merge-order assertion** nobody scored: "every relative link and anchor resolving — **including README's link to SP-17's `docs/security.md`, which is present on `develop` by the time this checkpoint runs** —". `plans/README.md:51` states verbatim that "**The wave 2–5 orders above are stated conventions, not enforced ones.**" and requires every wave from 2 onward to add its own build-order guard to `test/guards/buildorder_test.go` — no wave-5 guard is named anywhere in this file.
- The rewrite added three new `pluginmanifest.DefaultVersion` references but left **two pre-existing ones** uncorrected (now file lines 430 and 876), so the file names the forbidden symbol at five sites, only three of which are new. The extractor's framing implies the symbol enters with the rewrite; it does not.
- §5.1's B-E row (line 997, untouched by the rewrite) still issues `bench-hotpath … -n 200`, a flag the shipped harness rejects: `test/bench/hotpath/main.go:109-125` registers `iterations`, `hook`, `warm-daemon`, `json`, `project` and `under-coload`, and `flag.ContinueOnError` makes `-n 200` a parse failure. The same stale spelling survives at lines 284, 605 and 637 while eight other sites use `--iterations`. The paragraph the rewrite added immediately below tells the operator that a rejected flag "is the finding, not a command to rewrite" — which converts a plan defect into a false SP-17 failure.
- The rewrite declares `lint-windows` a required gate but adds **no verification step**: §1.17.19 and §4.2's closing sentence still name only SP-17's four jobs as the ones to confirm in branch protection, and ARCH §8:2128 names only `bench-gate` and `replay-gate` as required checks, so nothing in the spec settles it either.
- The new §5.1 paragraph mis-states the flag's value set as `--bundle native|universal`; SP-17 (`:1219`) specifies `native|universal|source` **defaulting to `source`** — and the omitted default is exactly what makes every pre-existing committed `bench-hotpath` invocation keep meaning a `go run` development number.

## Design conflicts

Twenty-six of the 425 change records carry a design conflict the verifier ruled real, after discarding the ones the extractors raised and the verifiers dismissed. They are not evenly spread: nine of the fifteen files carry at least one, six carry none, and `V4-SP-11-rehydrator-l5.md` alone carries six.

By counterparty, thirteen are against `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` — the normative interface spec — six against `Qompack.md`, four against `plans/TRACEABILITY.md` and three against `plans/README.md`. Two of the six Qompack conflicts (`SP-11-C12`, `SP-11-C8`) cite an architecture section as well, and two of the four traceability conflicts (`SP-15-C1`, `SP-15-C20`) also cite `Qompack.md:590`.

The structural fact that governs how to read all of them: **`Qompack.md` has exactly one commit in its history and was never touched by the rewrite.** `git log --oneline -- Qompack.md` returns one line, `d361f06`. So not one of these twenty-six is a design change. Every one is a plan-versus-spec divergence — a rewritten plan and a frozen or unamended document saying different things — and the only question each raises is which of the two is stale and what document has to move to close it.

That question does not always resolve against the plan. In seven records the verifier found the plan is right about the shipped tree and `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` is the stale party that was never amended: `SP-08-C2`, `SP-08-C6`, `SP-09-C09`, `SP-09-C10`, `SP-09-C21`, `SP-18-C10` and `SP-18-C18`. In one (`SP-14-C13`) the conflict is corrective — the *pre-rewrite* text contradicted the spec and the rewrite removes the contradiction. In two others (`SP-14-C4`, `SP-12-C6`) the plan invented or misread a rule the architecture does not carry. The pattern across the seven is identical and is itself the finding: the rewrite corrected the plans toward shipped code and opened no `arch/<reason>` amendment for the spec that still describes the old code.

But no §5.4 amendment is scheduled, so the divergence is live and silent |

### The eight that matter most

**`SP-08-C2` — `Options.Bind` is not in §5.** `grep -c "Bind" plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns **0**. §5.4's extension-seam note names three seams and only three: *"Handle registers an Op handler; the op-routing table is data, not a switch. / IdleController.Register adds O3/O5 background work. / Services is the late-bound dependency set"*, and the one wiring signature it freezes is line 807, `func (*Options) Handle(op ipc.Op, h ipc.Handler)`. The rewrite makes an undeclared method and an undeclared field set SP-08's sole wiring mechanism, calls it *"the sanctioned seam"*, and schedules no §5.4 amendment — Commit 0's checklist on `arch/sp08-observer-seams` amends only *"the §5.8 note in `plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md`"*. ARCH-4 states "Signatures are normative"; §0 states that "Silent divergence from §5 is the single failure mode that makes parallel waves worthless." The correction itself is right — the old text would have overwritten SP-05's five default routes, and `ingest` is unexported so the loss would be unrecoverable — which is exactly why the spec, not the plan, is what has to move. This conflict is also the root of the `XS-02` blocker below.

**`SP-08-C6` — `ArgsDigest` is bound to a §5.8 that does not carry it.** The plan states *"§5.8 ships `ArgsDigest(raw json.RawMessage) (core.Hash, string)`"* and lists it inside its block of `store.Store` interface methods. §5.8's `Store` interface, from line 1003, declares no such method; the only occurrence of the name in the entire file is the struct field `ArgsDigest   core.Hash` at line 988, and the shipped symbol is a package-level function at `internal/store/tooluseindex.go:348`. §5.8 also spells the cap in characters — line 989 declares ``ArgsPreview  string          // ≤120 chars, for tombstones and `timeline` `` — while both the plan and the shipped code cap at 120 **bytes**. The pre-step amends §5.8's `PutOptions` note and touches neither divergence, so SP-08 is bound to a contract §5.8 does not carry.

**`SP-09-C10` — §5.7 says `Load` writes the Loud line; the code says it writes nothing.** §5.7 line 905 declares `func Load(p string, s Sketch) error   // CRC + version checked; corrupt → ErrNotFound + Loud log`. The rewritten plan says the opposite in the same shape: *"`func Load(p string, s Sketch) error   // logs nothing — NOT for this call site`"*. The plan matches the tree — `Load` delegates to `LoadWithLog(p, s, logging.Nop())`, and `internal/sketch/io.go:85-86` states "no log LINE is written anywhere — not to the day log, not to LOUD.log" (the delegation itself is at `:95`). Worse than the annotation being stale: `grep -n 'ReplaceGenerational\|LoadWithLog\|HighestBloomBackupSeq\|ReplaceBloom' plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns nothing, and the plan now makes `LoadWithLog` and `ReplaceGenerational` mandatory. Under ARCH-4 that seam moved without an amendment landing on develop.

**`SP-11-C12` — the same rewrite amends §5.15 for a constant and silently widens a contract §5.15 also states.** `Qompack.md` §8.6 line 971 reads *"- Every nested `CLAUDE.md` in a directory containing a pointer-set file"*. `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.15 line 1603 — normative, unchanged in this rewrite — reads *"    // NestedClaudeMD returns CLAUDE.md files in directories containing a pointer-set file."* Both state the containing-directory-only reading. Only SP-01's implementation doc comment at `internal/rules/rules.go:32-33` and its conformance fixture say "or ancestor to", and the plan now instructs building the broader ancestor-walk behaviour on that doc comment's authority. ARCH-1 is explicit: "Where this document and `Qompack.md` disagree on *what* to build, `Qompack.md` wins." No §0 amendment to §5.15 and no PR against `Qompack.md` appears anywhere in the diff — unlike the `ItemKind` change in the same file, which does open one. The asymmetry is the defect.

**`SP-11-C24` — "against Phase 0 baseline" quietly became "against stock in this run".** `Qompack.md` §10 Phase 3's exit criterion reads verbatim at line 1097: *"**Exit criterion:** post-compaction first-divergence turn index improves against Phase 0 baseline at a *smaller* rehydration budget than stock; measured reduction in summarization-call input tokens when the span instruction is honoured."* "Phase 0 baseline" is the committed artifact — `test/replay/phases.go:60` names it via `baselinePolicyName = "stock"` and phase 0 computes it. The rewritten A2 compares `qompack-rehydrate` against the `stock` arm scored in the same run. That is a different quantity, and the difference is not academic: both arms can regress against the recorded Phase 0 number and A2 still passes. The plan argues in-run comparison is "stronger than reading a file", which is a defensible position and not the one `Qompack.md` states.

**`SP-14-C20` (with `SP-14-C14`) — the exit criteria stopped requiring what §5.17 names.** §5.17's line-1716 block states the `/qompack:status` surface normatively and per hook: *"`/qompack:status` output is the observability surface (G8.1): … hook latency p50/p99 **per hook** against budgets B-A/B-D …"*. `b1ce3b2` did two things in one commit. It conceded that on the daemon path — the path the plan says `status` prefers, *"because `obs.Registry` is daemon-resident, so the latency section is *only* real on this path"* — the per-hook rows render `no samples`, because the daemon's payload map carries only the seven budget clocks (`SP-14-C14`). And it rewrote the Definition-of-Done bullet that used to restate §5.17's "per hook" wording verbatim, dropping the phrase entirely (`SP-14-C20`). The result is that §5.17's per-hook element is satisfiable only on the disk-fallback path the plan itself calls degraded, and nothing in the exit criteria holds the slice to it. §0 is explicit that §5.17 is normative: "Every subplan … conforms to this document."

**`SP-18-C10` and `SP-18-C18` — SP-18 is right about the tree three times and amends nothing.** §8 line 2105 is unamended and normative: *"| `docs` | ubuntu | `devtool gen-config-docs`, `git diff --exit-code` — `docs/config-reference.md` can never drift from `config.Defaults()` |"*. SP-18's Mission item 2 now asserts *"It does *not* run `git diff --exit-code`"*, and `ci.yml:254-261` has exactly one step, so SP-18 is right; `ca63567` amended other §8 rows in the same merge and left this one, and the stale claim also propagates to `V1-SP-01:305`. The write-set change is the same story twice over. §13 invariant 7 at line 2492 reads *"**No network. No telemetry. No writes outside `.qompack/`** (plus `~/.qompack/` for the global layer)"*, while SP-18 now instructs the shipped docs to enumerate four prefixes — and instructs `docs/architecture.md` to quote invariant 7 verbatim, so one doc set would carry both. `internal/ipc/resolve.go:30-38` says four. And the same added paragraph ends *"there is no daemon registry file today, and the doc does not promise one"*, against line 449's *"Global, cross-project state lives in `~/.qompack/` (`config.json` user-global layer, `daemons.json` registry, `calibration.json`)"* — where `grep -rn 'daemons.json' internal/ cmd/` returns nothing. Three true statements about the tree, three unamended normative lines, no `arch/<reason>` commit.

**`V3-C5b` — V3-VERIFY reads README's guard obligation backwards.** `plans/README.md:51` states, verbatim: *"**Every wave from 2 onward adds its own build-order guard to `test/guards/buildorder_test.go` before its branches merge**, written and observed failing against the wrong order first, exactly as `TestGuard_Phase0BeforeStore` was; the wave's verification checkpoint names that guard. A wave that merges without one has an unverified order and must say so in its checkpoint report."* V3-VERIFY's §0a item 4 names README as the authoritative subplan decomposition for the wave-2 merge order — and then names no wave-2 guard, requires none to be authored, gives its §10 completion report no cell for the "unverified order" declaration, and reports the guard file's contents as evidence that nothing constrains the order. That is the exact opposite reading of the sentence. Both documents were rewritten in the same merge, three minutes apart (`1af5141` at 22:46:35, `b25d8d8` at 22:49:36), which is also how `V3-C4` came about: README's stated *reason* for the wave-2 order is refuted by V3-VERIFY's own §0a item 4 and was left standing. The cross-cutting agents found the same defect independently from two directions, as `WP-01`/`WP-03` and `CG-05`.

Per-record context — what each change actually did to its subplan, and the risk if it goes unnoticed — is in that subplan's section above, under the same id. Twenty-five of the twenty-six have a table row there; `SP-12-C6` is materiality-cosmetic, so it appears in V4-SP-12's prose rather than in its table. The strongest four also drive the recommended actions at the end of this document.

## Cross-cutting findings

Five agents audited what no single-file review can see: whether `plans/TRACEABILITY.md` is still true of the rewritten plans, whether the rewrites agree with each other and with the shipped tree, where wave-1's carried defects land, whether waves 2-5 have working process gates, and what the audit design itself left uncovered. They returned **64 findings — 5 blocker, 14 high, 24 medium, 16 low, 5 informational**. I re-verified all five blockers against the repository myself. Four hold as written (`XS-01`, `XS-02`, `CD-1`, `CD-2`). The fifth, `CG-01`, was a coverage complaint against the *earlier* synthesis pass and does not hold against this report's evidence set; it is downgraded to informational and dealt with under Open questions, leaving **four live blockers**.

The findings are not evenly distributed. Traceability produced no blocker and no high: `TRACEABILITY.md` survived a 4,656-line rewrite substantially intact, and its defects are ownership-attribution imprecision. Everything severe is in the other three areas — two plans authoring the same file with contradictory bodies, a wiring function with no caller, fourteen carried defects with no working disposition path, and three checkpoint documents that invoke gates with flags the tools reject.

### Traceability integrity

`plans/TRACEABILITY.md` has exactly two commits in its history and was not rewritten in `c0c5627`, so every finding here is a question of whether the rewrites moved out from under it. The headline claims survive: all 40 gap ids exist, each appears in its assigned owning plan, and no gap is doubly owned. What fails is the preamble's method claim — "ownership below is taken from each subplan's own header metadata" holds for §1 and not for §3 — and six rows where the design document assigns a gap or phase across two subplans and `TRACEABILITY` records one.

### Cross-subplan contract consistency

This area checked the eleven rewritten subplans against each other and against `internal/`, `cmd/`, `test/` and `tools/` as shipped. Most of the round's corrections are sound — the "bind, not handle" theme is applied correctly in SP-10, SP-11, SP-13 and SP-14, and the SP-13 daemon-bootstrap ownership negotiation is reciprocated by SP-12. The failures are where two plans were rewritten by different agents in the same batch and neither read the other: one shared file with contradictory bodies, one unreconciled interface, and one shipped-clamp finding that SP-11 discovered and SP-15 did not inherit 68 seconds later.

**XS-01 (blocker) — two plans, one file, two incompatible bodies.** SP-12 (wave 3) marks `internal/scheduler/formulas.go` **delete** and creates `skirental.go` taking `SkiRentalShouldWrite` over "adding the `w <= 0` guard — a declared behaviour change", with the body `if r <= 0 || w <= 0 { return false }; return expectedReads > w/r` and a Done-checklist grep for exactly one declaration. SP-16 (wave 5) opens from the contrary premise: "SP-01 shipped the closed form in `internal/scheduler/formulas.go` … not a stub", then instructs Commit 2 to "**Move** `SkiRentalShouldWrite` … out of `internal/scheduler/formulas.go` into `skirental.go`" and to "Create `internal/scheduler/skirental.go`", expecting the first test run to "**fail to compile**". At the wave-3 tag SP-16 claims to start from, `formulas.go` is gone and `skirental.go` exists. SP-16 also asserts "`formulas.go` keeps `YoungDaly`, `pSelectionAvailable` and `PSelectionAvailable` untouched" — SP-12 redistributes those to `youngdaly.go` and `gate.go` and removes the file. The bodies then disagree on behaviour: SP-16 routes through `SkiRentalThreshold(r, w)`, which returns `+Inf` only on `r <= 0 || IsNaN(r) || IsNaN(w)`, so at `w = 0` the threshold is 0 and `SkiRentalShouldWrite` reports **true** — reinstating exactly the "always write for a cache write that cannot be free" behaviour SP-12 declared it was fixing, and failing SP-12's pinned `w=0 → false` row. I read SP-16's own test table (`:1539-1550`): six `TestSkiRentalShouldWrite` rows, none with `w = 0` and none with `r = 0`, so nothing in SP-16 would catch the regression. The audit made this worse rather than better: the pre-audit SP-16 carried a tolerant "Delete SP-01's stub body of `SkiRentalShouldWrite` if it lives elsewhere; there must be exactly one definition" (`229906f^` line 1620), which SP-12's version would have satisfied; `229906f` replaced it with a hard-coded move out of a file that will not exist.

**XS-02 (blocker) — the wiring function nobody calls.** The `0380e66` rewrite moved SP-08 from registering five ops to binding five `Services` seams through `func WireObserver(o *Options, s *SessionRegistry) (observer.Observer, error)`, and in the same commit hardened the branch-purity criteria. The spec says only "Called from the daemon's construction path once Options are complete" and names no call site. The Done checklist says `feat/sp08-observer-l0` "contains no edit to `internal/store`, `internal/cli` or `internal/daemon/handlers.go`" and that "Only one file outside `internal/observer` and the test trees was added on this branch: `internal/daemon/observer_ops.go`" — `internal/daemon/daemon.go` appears in no file-map row. I re-ran the two greps: `grep -rn 'daemon\.New(' --include=*.go internal/ cmd/ test/ tools/ | grep -v _test.go` returns exactly one line, `internal/cli/daemon.go:86` — the file SP-08 is forbidden to touch — and `grep -rn 'WireObserver' plans/*.md` returns hits only in `V3-SP-08`, `V3-VERIFY` and `V6-VERIFY`; SP-11, SP-12, SP-13, SP-14 and SP-15 each specify their own composition-root wiring and none calls it. Every other wave-2+ subplan names its wiring site explicitly. At SP-08's merge the five `Services` seams stay nil, every hook still ACKs, and the failure is silent; `V3-VERIFY` H12's `TestE2E_ObserverThroughDaemon` is the row that fails.

**XS-03 (high) — the SessionStart seam has two spellings.** SP-11 states the seam is "declared by SP-08" and pins `type SessionStartRehydrator interface { Compact(...) (hookio.Output, error); Clear(...) error }`. SP-08 declares `type Rehydrator interface { OnCompact(...) (hookio.Output, error); OnClear(...) (hookio.Output, error) }`, declares `Options.Rehydrate Rehydrator`, calls `o.opt.Rehydrate.OnCompact`/`.OnClear` in its `OnSessionStart` switch, and names its tests against `OnCompact`. Three divergences: the type name, both method names, and `Clear`/`OnClear`'s return type. SP-11's Done checklist asserts `var _ observer.SessionStartRehydrator = NewRehydrateService(...)` compiles, which cannot hold. The shipped tree settles nothing — `internal/observer` declares no such interface and `observer.go:62-81`'s `Options` has no rehydrator field — so an implementer has only the two plans. The mismatch predates the audit, but both files were rewritten this round and neither reconciled it; `e18aaac` rewrote SP-11's wiring section wholesale and hedged with a generic "if the seam that landed on `develop` differs in name or signature, resolve it by the §0 amendment procedure", which converts a documentation defect into an unnecessary arch branch.

**XS-04 (high) — the same shipped clamp, discovered once and not carried across.** SP-15's redundancy detector enumerates candidates with `s.Search(ctx, store.Query{Since: time.Time{}, K: o.MaxCandidates})` at `MaxCandidates` 5000 and states "SP-06's `Search` ranks and caps at `K`". I confirmed the shipped behaviour: `internal/store/search.go:38-42` declares `maxK = 100` and `maxCandidates = 512`, `:60-66` clamps `case k > maxK: k = maxK`, `:101-104` truncates, `:231-236` sorts TS-descending. SP-15 therefore receives at most the 100 most recent records of the *project*, not the session's 5000. This is precisely the finding SP-11's rewrite (`e18aaac`) wrote up — "`FSStore.Search` clamps `K` to `maxK = 100` … returns the 100 **most recent** prompts" — before dropping `Search` from its own path. SP-15's rewrite (`681cd9a`) landed 68 seconds later without inheriting it. SP-15's own guard cannot fire: `TestDetectRedundancyEnumeratesWholeSession` puts 12 records in one session and asserts all 12 return, well under the clamp, so the stated remedy — an `arch/store-tooluses-by-session` amendment adding `ToolUsesBySession` — is never triggered by the test that exists to trigger it.

### Carried defects and deferred obligations landing on wave 2+

Wave 1 closed with sixteen recorded defects in `plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` — two `fixed`, fourteen `deferred:V3-VERIFY`. This area asked whether V3-VERIFY can actually dispose of them. It cannot: eight of the fourteen are named in no wave-2+ document at all, the sign-off gate that would block on them is keyed to a file V3-VERIFY never tells anyone to write, and the one sentence in V3-VERIFY describing the guard's blocking rule states the semantics the A7 fix removed. Separately, three V3-VERIFY gate rows assert numbers that carried defects already record as unmet, with no cross-reference in either direction.

**CD-1 (blocker) — eight deferrals with no home.** I re-ran the counts. `grep -c 'deferred:V3-VERIFY' plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` returns **14**: SP04-D2, SP04-D3, SP04-D5, SP04-D6, SP04-D7, SP06-D1, SP06-D2, SP05-D1, SP02-D1 through SP02-D6. `plans/V3-VERIFY-observer-and-negative-knowledge.md:62` opens "**Six carried-defect rows are `deferred:V3-VERIFY` in `plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv`, and the guard makes ignoring them fail**" and enumerates six — SP04-D2, SP04-D3, SP04-D5, SP04-D6, SP06-D1, SP05-D1. Per-id grep over that whole file returns **0 hits** for each of SP04-D7, SP06-D2 and SP02-D1..D6. No later checkpoint mentions the manifest at all: `grep -n 'CARRIED-DEFECTS' plans/V3-VERIFY-*.md plans/V4-VERIFY*.md plans/V5-VERIFY*.md plans/V6-VERIFY*.md` returns one line, line 62. The cause is sequencing, not judgement: the eight rows were added by `5b418a0` ("docs(plans): carry the eight findings this round did not close"), and the newest commit touching V3-VERIFY is `b25d8d8`, an ancestor of the `c0c5627` merge and therefore earlier. Nothing re-opened V3-VERIFY afterwards.

**CD-2 (blocker) — the gate is keyed to a file the plan never writes.** `TestCarriedDefects_WaveReportRequiresResolution` keys on exactly one path: `report := filepath.Join(root, "plans", wave+"-report.md")`, and `if _, err := os.Stat(report); err != nil { continue }` — if the file is absent the assertion is vacuous. `ls plans/*-report.md` returns `V1-report.md` and `V2-report.md` only, so the gate covers zero of the fourteen rows today. That would be fine if V3-VERIFY told the session to write it; §8.5 says the opposite, and says it *after* the `git merge --no-ff` / `git tag v0.2.0` block: "Record the completion report (§8.5's table below) in the merge commit body or as `docs/adr/0100-v3-verification.md`". Neither destination is `plans/V3-report.md`. V1-VERIFY got this right — "Commit the filled-in report to `verify/v1` as `plans/V1-report.md` before merging" (`:859`) — and V4-VERIFY has already replicated the defect at `:1410`, with V5-VERIFY and V6-VERIFY naming no output path at all. A V3 session following its own plan literally merges and tags wave 2 with all fourteen rows still `deferred:V3-VERIFY` and trips no test.

**CD-3 (high) — the document states the semantics the fix round removed.** §0a item 5 closes: "`test/guards/carrieddefects_test.go` blocks `plans/V3-report.md` on any row left **`open`**". That is the pre-A7 rule. The shipped `unresolved()` is `d.status == "open" || strings.HasPrefix(d.status, deferredPrefix)`, and the guard's own comment records why it changed: "Until the 2026-08-22 audit this was `status == \"open\"` alone, and every row in the shipped manifest is `fixed` or `deferred:V3-VERIFY` — so the evidence-liveness check below covered zero rows, the sign-off gate covered zero rows". No row in the manifest is `open`. A V3 session reading item 5 literally concludes the gate cannot fire on anything and that the rows are advisory — and, combined with CD-2, would be right in practice for the wrong reason.

**CD-5 (high) — the checkpoint asserts numbers its own carried defects record as unmet.** §6.3's component micro-budgets list `store — Put cold / warm | ≤ 3 ms / ≤ 400 µs` and `store — GC 50k objects / MarkEncoded 100 | ≤ 2 s (deadline ±50 ms) / ≤ 1 ms` as pass criteria. SP06-D2 says of exactly those two numbers that they "are 9x and 19x over on Windows and have never been measured on the reference platform"; `V2-SP-06:890` records 7.68 ms warm and `:997` records 27.2 ms cold. SP06-D1 says `GCReport.Duration` "can overshoot the deadline by the whole tombstone phase (100-260 ms measured at 650 dead roots)", which cannot satisfy "deadline ±50 ms". Neither §6.3 nor the §10 report template mentions either row, and SP06-D2 is one of CD-1's eight orphans — so a V3 session on Windows hits a red row with no pointer to the record that already adjudicated it.

**CD-6 (high) — an instruction that contradicts a deferred finding.** §3.2's Belady step reads: "`oracle` must score exactly `1.0` on all 24 sessions and `null` exactly `0.0`; `stock` strictly between. **If `stock` ties `null`, the corpus is wrong — fix the corpus, never the assertion.**" SP02-D3, deferred to this checkpoint, already says the corpus is wrong in that direction: "the Belady keep budget never binds on the corpus (oracle `rewrite_span_tokens` is 0 at every event), so `fraction_of_opt` grades against a trivial ceiling". SP02-D1 and SP02-D2 bear on the same section's metrics, and SP02-D4/D5/D6 on pMin's scope, `decision_preservation`'s horizon and the stock model's host padding. None of the six appears in §3.2, §2 group B, §6 or §10. `V2-report.md` §16.8 explicitly assigns this work here — a corpus change "re-baselines every metric for every policy at once … which is a checkpoint's work" — and V3-VERIFY does not know.

**CD-7 (high) — the fix round's contract changes reached no wave-2+ plan.** Both changes recorded in `V2-report.md` §16.8.1 land only in V2 documents. Four consequences follow. (a) V3-VERIFY §X5 runs the driver with `--sketch $TMP/v3-health.json` and no `--baseline`; `test/replay/main.go:50` sets `defaultBaselinePath = "testdata/baseline/phase0.json"` and `:147` defaults the flag to it, so X5 silently compares real ledger health against the baseline's watch-fors — now real values judged as ratios at 2% (`gate_test.go:306-309` pins `bloom_fp_rate` 0.006 and `bloom_fill_ratio` 0.18, `gate.go:65-68` marks both `DirLowerBetter`). That is exactly the comparison `cb27449` had to disable with `--baseline ""`, "blocked at +188 % and +69 %". X5's negative control is unattributable for the same reason. (b) F10 and §6.3 still name only `TestGC_DeadlineTruncatesAndResumes`; the two tests pinning the new split (`internal/store/gc_test.go:1494`, `:1546`) appear in no V3+ document. (c) `V5-SP-14:1065` still claims the GC dry-run probe is "hard-bounded at 250 ms by `GCPolicy.Deadline` … **by construction**", which `gcrun.go`'s `gcBudget.cancelled` — deliberately ctx-only for the mark's index walks — makes false. (d) `V4-SP-12:2361` says deriving `GCPolicy.Deadline` from ctx makes the idle `gc` task "resumable … rather than truncated"; post-fix a deadline smaller than the mark harvest returns `GCReport{Truncated: true}` with nothing collected and no cursor persisted (`gcrun.go:93-101`).

### Wave process obligations and gates for waves 2-5

Merge orders, build-order guards, bench and replay invocations, the §11.3 2% rule and the §6.4 coverage floors. The substance is mostly sound — wave-2, wave-3 and wave-5 merge orders agree across documents, the 2% rule is quoted identically in all four checkpoints including its boundary arithmetic, and every coverage floor but one matches `OWNERS.tsv`. What fails is executability: three checkpoints invoke the replay driver and the hotpath bench with flags the tools reject outright, and the audit's own new per-wave build-order-guard obligation was written into `README.md` and into no checkpoint that owes it.

| WP-11 | medium | V6-VERIFY's Rule W-1 skip check greps `internal/checkpoint/checkpointtest/` but asserts three suites, one of which (`RunPinsSuite`) lives in `internal/pins/pinstest`; the suite that is there (`RunTruncateSuite`) is not named | `V6-VERIFY:287`; `internal/pins/pinstest/suite.go:28`; `internal/checkpoint/checkpointtest/suite.go:79,115,161`; `V4-SP-10:1322` flags the trap; `V5-VERIFY:124` shows the correct form | `V6-VERIFY:287` |
| WP-12 | low | V5-VERIFY permits the verify branch to sign off its own 2% regression; V3, V4 and V6 forbid it, V4 in as many words | `V5-VERIFY:997-1000`; `V3-VERIFY:1274-1277`; `V4-VERIFY:1228-1231` ("a checkpoint that signs off its own regression has defeated its purpose"); `V6-VERIFY:1092-1101` | `V5-VERIFY:997-1000` |
| WP-13 | low | V5-VERIFY's eight-guard inventory names neither `TestGuard_SubmodularDefaultsOff` nor the name SP-15 renames it to, so a deletion rather than a re-point would read as green | `V5-SP-15:317`, `:2264`, `:2553`; `V5-VERIFY:126`; `test/guards/buildorder_test.go:112` | `V5-VERIFY:126` |
| WP-14 | info | V6-VERIFY attributes the wave-5 merge order to `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §9, which fixes no order. The order itself is right and independently justified | `V6-VERIFY:13`, `:19`; `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §9, `:2524`; `README.md:46` | `V6-VERIFY:13` |

**WP-01 (high) — an obligation with no owner.** `plans/README.md:51` states, verbatim: "**Every wave from 2 onward adds its own build-order guard to `test/guards/buildorder_test.go` before its branches merge**, written and observed failing against the wrong order first, exactly as `TestGuard_Phase0BeforeStore` was; the wave's verification checkpoint names that guard. A wave that merges without one has an unverified order and must say so in its checkpoint report." Grepping the plan set for that language returns two hits: README itself, and `V3-VERIFY:52` — where it is the opposite of a schedule. §0a item 4 uses the guard's absence as an argument that nothing needs doing: "`test/guards/buildorder_test.go` holds no wave-2 guard at all; its six guards are … So README's order stands, §0 below matches it, and **no document needs fixing**." No V3-VERIFY row authors a guard, names one, or records the wave as having an unverified order. V4, V5 and V6-VERIFY do not mention the obligation at all; their build-order rows re-run the existing set, and V4-VERIFY's completion line is a history inspection ("Wave-3 merge order confirmed: SP-10 → SP-11 → SP-12 → SP-13"), not a guard. The obligation was created by the audit itself — `1af5141`, whose message reads "Every wave from 2 onward now owes its own guard, observed failing against the wrong order first, named in that wave's checkpoint" — and no companion commit propagated it. Four waves owe a guard, no checkpoint asks for one, and no checkpoint carries the fallback declaration.

**WP-03 (high) — the merge order's stated reason is false.** README justifies the wave-2 row with "SP-09's elimination ledger lands before SP-08's observer, **which imports it (§3.2)**". SP-08 does not import `negknow` and is forbidden to: `V3-SP-08:517-519` says "SP-08 declines two of them — `chunk` … and `negknow`, because §8.1 item 5 forbids L0 from touching the Bloom filter", its Done checklist at `:2473` forbids the import outright, and `V6-VERIFY:245` makes the exclusion an exit criterion ("**no** `scheduler`, `checkpoint`, `symbols`, `contract`, **`negknow`**"). V3-VERIFY says it plainly at `:50-52`: "`internal/observer` does **not** import `internal/negknow` … `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.2's allow-table … merely *permits* the edge; a permission is not a dependency". The order may still be right, but its only stated reason is false, and it is the reason a future wave author reads first. The audit wrote both halves — `1af5141` the claim, `b25d8d8` the refutation, three minutes apart — without reconciling them. This is the same defect TR-04 reaches from the traceability side.

**WP-04 (high) — two documents, two wave-4 orders, one of them executable.** `README.md:45` gives "Wave 4: SP-15 → SP-16 → SP-14". `V5-VERIFY:7-13` gives the reverse — "in the merge order fixed by `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §9 … 1. `feat/sp14-slash-commands-and-observability` 2. `feat/sp15-analyzer-selection-and-grammar` 3. `feat/sp16-phase7-refinements`". Two defects compound. The cited authority carries no order: `00-ARCHITECTURE.md` §9 says only "all of that wave's branches merge into `develop` **in the stated order** (§ merge strategy in the subplan decomposition)", and §14 at `:2524` delegates the same way — this is the V2-MERGE-21 failure class verbatim, and V3-VERIFY §0a item 4 settled the identical question for wave 2 by naming README authoritative. And the disagreement is executable, not cosmetic: `V5-VERIFY:24` instructs `git log --oneline --merges -3 # must show the three wave-4 merges, in the order above`, so a `develop` merged per README fails V5's first gate. Nothing breaks the tie — SP-14, SP-15 and SP-16 each list the other two only under "Runs in parallel with", and none is another's prerequisite.

**WP-05 (high) — six gate rows that cannot parse their own flags.** I enumerated `test/replay`'s flags directly from `main.go:146-165`: `corpus baseline policies out signoff growth sketch phase regen-corpus write-baseline max-cpu max-wall ci`. There is no `--json`, no `--filter`, no `--set`, and the driver takes no positional arguments. Six rows use them anyway: `V4-VERIFY:403` (`--filter multi-compaction --set checkpoint.frontier.advanceOnSegmentClose=false --json …`), `V5-VERIFY:315` (identical), `V5-VERIFY:989` and its §6.3 block, and `V6-VERIFY:286`, `:599-601`, `:1097`. This is not an undiscovered fact — `V4-SP-10:1475` states it exactly ("There is no `--filter`, no `--set` and no `--json`; the driver parses with `flag.ContinueOnError`, so any of those three is `flag provided but not defined` and exit 2") and `V4-VERIFY:244` repeats the warning 159 lines before violating it. All three broken rows claim to re-run SP-10's own frontier residual-span exit gate, so that criterion is un-runnable at V4, V5 and V6 as written.

**WP-06 (high) — a rejected flag, fixed in one checkpoint of three.** `test/bench/hotpath/main.go:138-146` defines `hookArgs`, which accepts only `""` and `"observe-tool"` and otherwise returns `fmt.Errorf("--hook %q not supported (only observe-tool)", hook)`; `:132` validates before any measurement runs, so the failure is exit 2 with no JSON rather than a missed budget. Commit `dafc5bf` corrected V5 and said why in its own message — "`--hook checkpoint` is not a thing … B-E is measured on every run" — and `V5-VERIFY:887` now carries the correction verbatim. The siblings were not fixed: `V4-VERIFY:302`, `:402`, `:1000`, `:1059` and `V6-VERIFY:284`, `:605`, `:997` still build six B-E rows on the rejected flag. B-E (`checkpoint_finalize` p99 < 2 s) is a hard-fail budget, and it is un-measurable as specified at both V4 and V6.

## What was checked and found sound

The audit proved as much as it found. Recording it matters because several of these are the claims a reader would otherwise assume the findings above had undermined.

**Traceability (area 1) proved the gap ledger itself is intact.**

- The 40-gap set is exactly right. `grep -ohE "G[0-9]+\.[0-9]+"` over `plans/*.md` and over `Qompack.md` each return the same 40 distinct ids; no stray or malformed id exists anywhere in `plans/`.
- Every one of the 40 ids appears in its `TRACEABILITY`-assigned owning plan, with counts recorded per id, and **no gap is doubly owned**: the `**Gaps closed:**` headers of all eighteen plans partition the 40 ids exactly once each and match §1's owner column row for row. Every prose occurrence that would look like a second claim was checked individually and is inside a `>` blockquote of `Qompack.md`.
- The rewrite did not touch the metadata `TRACEABILITY` derives ownership from: for all eleven wave-2+ plans, `**Gaps closed:**` and `**Design sections:**` are byte-identical to `d361f06`.
- All 8 MCP tools are SP-13's and all 7 slash commands are SP-14's, the latter matching `Qompack.md` §7.5's manifest exactly. Nine of the eleven §5 revision-item obligations are carried by their named owners (the two exceptions are TR-07's id-only orphans). Every phase's exit criterion is present in its owning plan, Phase 7's §11.3 substitute included.
- §8's honesty surface holds — all four named tests exist in SP-18, which commits to the §12 bullet list "**verbatim, byte-for-byte**" — as do §9's four closing-note priorities and §4's `PreCompact`, `SessionStart`, `Stop`/`SubagentStop` assignments.
- `TRACEABILITY.md` has exactly two commits in its history, and the second changes one parenthetical. The premise that a 4,656-line rewrite left it untouched is confirmed.

**Contract consistency (area 2) cleared eight of the nine rewrite commits it examined.**

- `b1ce3b2` (SP-14): the new `status.full` op collides with nothing — `ipc` ships thirteen ops, `Op.Valid()` is called from no non-test shipped code, and `dispatchOp` is a plain map lookup. SP-14's stated reason for not re-registering `ipc.OpStatus` verifies: `Options.Handle` replaces registrations, `buildRoutes` prefers them, and `test/bench/hotpath/measure.go` decodes `daemon.StatusSnapshot` over that op for B-B. Its pluginmanifest "extend, never duplicate" claim is accurate field for field.
- `229906f` (SP-16): SP-06 really does reserve the segment Bloom record in code, with the doc comment naming SP-16 as the writer, and SP-16's anchored-edit facts — `segLog`'s field list, `openSegLog`'s signature, its two call sites, and `Close` appending before mutating — all check out. There is no ski-rental stub; `formulas.go` holds a real closed form pinned by two real tests.
- `e18aaac` (SP-11): dropping `Store.Search` orphans no `TRACEABILITY` obligation, and the replacement path through `Store.ToolUse(ctx, firstPromptID(session))` against SP-08's `VerbatimPromptID` is consistent between the two plans.
- `74c2c58` (SP-10): giving up `ipc.OpCheckpoint` is correct — the shipped `handleCheckpoint` does the History observation, terminal-hook marker, B-E timing and `Services.PreCompact` call that an Options-side re-registration would have deleted — and no other subplan expects SP-10 to own that route.
- `121c8ec` (SP-12): deleting the coupling cache orphans nothing SP-07 ships; `CrossingEdges` stays live and is two binary searches, not a graph walk, and the §8.4 `segment_coupling` obligation is still discharged.
- `73dbd4e` (SP-13): the daemon-bootstrap ownership claim is consistent with SP-05 as shipped and reciprocated by SP-12 in three places, and README does place SP-13 last in wave 3.
- `0380e66` / `de7f7b1`: SP-08 and SP-09 still own everything `TRACEABILITY` assigns them, in substance as well as in header.
- Two whole-set cross-greps came back clean: of 174 file/subplan spec pairs across the eleven subplans, exactly one path is doubly claimed (XS-01), and no two plans define a shared `runtime.<group>.<key>` differently.

**Carried defects (area 3) proved the manifest and its guard are structurally sound — it is the checkpoint text that is wrong.**

- `plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` is well formed: 16 data rows, six tab-separated columns each, unique ids, every status in `{fixed, deferred:V3-VERIFY}`, no self-deferral, and every owner and deferral target resolving to a real checkpoint document.
- Every one of the 16 ids has a `## <id>` section in exactly the document the guard's derivation rule selects, verified per row by reimplementing `carriedDefectsDocFor`. Both non-`-` evidence tests exist at the lines cited. `go test ./test/guards/ -run 'TestCarriedDefects' -count=1` passes on the current tree.
- `plans/V3-report.md` is correctly absent rather than missing, and the sign-off gate is vacuous by design in that state — confirmed empirically by creating and removing a throwaway file, with `git status --porcelain plans/` clean afterwards.
- The wave-1 bindings that carried defects into SP-08 and SP-09 survived the rewrite: `b7d9654`'s sections are byte-identical apart from one corrected commit citation, and SP-09's grew a third inherited-constraint item with a named pinning test. The remapped sha `666b120` is real and is the commit its citation claims.
- B-G landed in shipped code exactly as V3-VERIFY describes it, and both contract changes `V2-report.md` §16.8.1 records are present in shipped code. V3-VERIFY's B10 lists 19 replay-gate test names and all 19 exist; row B11 is safe under the changed bloom-ceiling contract because it compares the baseline against the fixture it was recorded from.

**Wave process (area 4) proved three of the four merge orders and the whole of the 2% rule.**

- Wave-2, wave-3 and wave-5 merge orders agree between README and their checkpoints. Wave 2's was settled by V3-VERIFY on the correct mechanics — §14 delegates to the subplan decomposition, which is README — closing V2-MERGE-21 for that wave; wave 3's carries a real per-branch justification; wave 5's has an independent reason from SP-18's own header. Only wave 4 disagrees (WP-04), and only wave 2's *rationale* is false (WP-03).
- The two shipped build-order guards behave as README describes: `TestGuard_Phase0BeforeStore` fires exactly on the SP-02-before-SP-06 constraint, and `TestGuard_StoreAndNegknowBeforeCheckpoint` is genuinely cross-wave and therefore discriminates no permutation of wave 3.
- The 2% rule's substance is stated consistently everywhere. All four wave-2+ checkpoints quote §11.3 verbatim and identically, all state the boundary as exclusive (−1.98% passes, −2.02% fails), and V3 and V4 reproduce the gate arithmetic identically including the zero-baseline absolute-tolerance branch. Only sign-off *eligibility* differs (WP-12).
- Coverage floors match `OWNERS.tsv` for every wave-2+ package except `pins`: negknow 90, observer 75, checkpoint 90, rehydrate 85, rules 75, skills 75, scheduler 85, mcp 85, commands 75, analyzer 85, grammar 75, each asserted at the same number by every checkpoint that names it. The stub-exemption sets are also correct for their waves — V3-VERIFY's ten and V4-VERIFY's three are exactly the right `OWNERS.tsv` rows.
- V3-VERIFY §7.3 and V4-VERIFY §6.3 invoke the replay gate correctly, with `--phase`, `--growth`, `--sketch`, `--signoff`, `--max-cpu` and `--out`, matching CI's replay-gate job. These two blocks are the working template WP-05 and WP-07 depart from. The `runpatterns` and `docmarkers` lints both pass on the tree as shipped, with two legitimate documented waivers, and no unfilled placeholder survives anywhere in `plans/`.
- No `bench-gate` or `replay-gate` devtool task is missing: those are CI job names, and every command the plans actually invoke exists.

**The completeness critic (area 5) independently re-verified several load-bearing claims.** `grep -c "Bind" plans/00-ARCHITECTURE.md` returns 0; `internal/cli/daemon.go:86` is the only non-test `daemon.New` call site and no plan but SP-08 and the VERIFY documents mentions `WireObserver`; commit `666b120` exists and contains `TestKnownDeletionMediatedLimit`. It also confirmed that all eleven wave-2+ commit plans stayed inside `00-ARCHITECTURE` §10's 5-8 band after the rewrites (only SP-08 moved, 7 → 8, with the eighth declared out-of-band by the plan itself); that the code-fix round removed or renamed **no** test function, so no wave-2+ plan cites a deleted test; that the `contract.ID` set is unchanged, so V4-VERIFY's and SP-18's nine-id lists are not stale; that `Qompack.md` has never been modified; that package ownership has no orphans (33 on-disk internal packages, 33 `OWNERS.tsv` rows, one-to-one); that no wave-2+ subplan lists a same-wave sibling as a prerequisite; and that the ten NodeID constructor signatures the un-audited `00-ARCHITECTURE` rewrite added are all real.

## Open questions and unverified claims

The completeness critic's own first finding was that the original synthesis pass was coverage-limited: **only 1 of the 15 per-file records reached it** — every per-file object carried `"file":"V3-SP-08-observer-l0.md"` — with 14 of the 15 files unrepresented, and two records truncated mid-sentence. (Its line arithmetic, "3,454 of the 4,414 inserted lines", does not reproduce: the fifteen files carry 3,776 insertions / 5,342 changed lines in `c0c5627`. The coverage claim does not depend on it.) That finding was correct about the material it was given, and it is the reason this report exists. **This report is the re-run on the complete evidence set.** I verified that directly: `merged.json` carries 15 top-level per-file keys — all eleven wave-2+ subplans and all four checkpoint documents — and `counts.json` records `filesAudited: 15`, 425 changes extracted, 92 more caught by verifiers, 0 REFUTED. The truncations are also repaired; `XS-08`, one of the two records the critic found cut off, now terminates cleanly. `CG-01` therefore **does not hold against this report** and is downgraded from blocker to informational. It stands as a record of the earlier pass, not as a live defect.

What follows is what this audit did **not** establish.

|---|---|---|---|---|

Neither are `OWNERS.tsv`, `README.md`, `TRACEABILITY.md` or `V2-report.md` | `git show --stat ca63567`, subject "docs(arch): correct the normative spec against shipped code"; the §5.9 NodeID constructor table it adds is labelled "(SP-11 amendment)" and is what SP-08-C3 rests on | `00-ARCHITECTURE.md`; the basis of SP-08-C2/C3/C6 and TR-13 |
| CG-03 | high | Restates CD-1/CD-3 from the process side: V3-VERIFY says six deferred rows, the manifest now carries fourteen, and the A7 fix changed the guard's blocking rule under it | `V3-VERIFY:62-73`; `awk -F'\t' '/deferred:V3-VERIFY/{print $1}'` → 14 ids; `git show c0c5627:plans/CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv \| grep -c` → 6 | `V3-VERIFY`; `CARRIED-DEFECTS.tsv` |

Same defect as WP-01, from the process side | `1af5141` (22:46:35) vs `b25d8d8` (22:49:36); `V3-VERIFY:43-57`; the three sibling checkpoints' guard rows | `README.md:51`; all four checkpoints |

Three of the eight need a design ruling | the eight `"kind":"missed-by-extractor"` objects; their subjects include a rewritten resolved decision, three SP-08 tests placed in SP-05's packages, and the Commit 1 TDD qualification | `V3-SP-08`; the treatment of those eight items |
